Pehle i.i.d. assumption theek theek likhein. Phir teeno ke liye batayein ke kaunsa hissa tootta hai aur ilaaj kya hai:

(a) User ka agla gaana predict karna
(b) Haath se likhe digits — training American postal codes par, test Pakistani par
(c) Ek hi gali ke gharon ka sailaab ka khatra

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Assumption: training and test examples are drawn independently from the same distribution — two separate claims.

(a) Next song → independence fails: consecutive plays are sequentially dependent (and that dependence is the signal); tastes also drift. Prescription: sequence modelling + temporal validation, never random.

(b) US → Pakistan digits → identical distribution fails: handwriting conventions, pen, paper, scanner differ. Task unchanged → covariate shift. Prescription: transfer learning — fine-tune on Pakistani samples, augment; validate on Pakistani data only.

(c) Same street → independence fails: flood risk is spatially autocorrelated (shared elevation, drainage). Prescription: collective classification, and split by street/catchment, not by house.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Assumption: training aur test ke points (1) ek doosre se bay-niyaaz (independent) aur (2) ek hi jagah se (same distribution) uthaye gaye hain. Ye do alag daawe hain — aur dono alag alag toot sakte hain.

(a) Agla gaana → independence tootta hai.

Kyun: gaane ek doosre se jure hue hain — jo banda abhi Nusrat sun raha hai, uska agla gaana bhi ghaaliban qawwali hoga. Aur mazay ki baat: yehi juraav asal signal hai, isi se to andaza lagta hai! Ilaaj: sequence modelling, aur validation temporal (waqt ke hisaab se) — random hargiz nahi, warna model banday ke baad ke gaane dekh kar pehle ka andaza lagayega.

(b) US digits par train, Pakistani par test → identical distribution tootta hai.

Kyun: hum log 1, 4 aur 7 American tareeqe se nahi likhte. Pen, kaaghaz, scanner — sab alag. Yaani test ka data doosri jagah se aaya. Magar kaam wohi hai (digit pehchanna), is liye isay covariate shift kehte hain. Ilaaj: transfer learning — Pakistani samples par fine-tune karein, aur validation sirf Pakistani data par.

(c) Ek hi gali ke ghar → independence tootta hai.

Kyun: ek gali ke gharon ki zameen ki bulandi ek jaisi hai, paani ka nikaas ek jaisa hai. Agar ek ghar mein paani aaya to parosi ke mein bhi aayega. Yaani ye ghar azaad points nahi, jure hue hain. Ilaaj: collective classification, aur validation ke liye data gali / ilaaqe ke hisaab se baantein, ghar ke hisaab se nahi.

Yaad rakhein — do sire, do ilaaj: points ek doosre se jure hon → collective classification. Data ki jagah badal jaaye → transfer learning.

P-2.66 marks

Why does training error always fall as flexibility rises while test error does not? Why does this make training error useless for hyper-parameters? Give two hyper-parameters it would choose wrongly.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(a) Model ki lachak (flexibility) barhate jaayein to training error hamesha girta hai — magar test error nahi. Kyun?
(b) Is wajah se training error se hyper-parameters chunna kyun bay-kaar hai?
(c) Do aise hyper-parameters batayein jo wo ghalat chunega.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Training error falls monotonically because more flexibility enlarges the hypothesis space, and the best element of a larger set can never be worse than the best element of a subset. In the limit it reaches 0.

Test error does not, because it has two opposing parts: as flexibility rises bias falls but variance rises (the fit tracks this sample's noise, which will not recur). Hence the U-shape with an interior minimum.

Why useless for selection: being monotone, optimising it always picks the most flexible setting — i.e. maximum overfitting. It contains no information about the variance term, which is the whole thing we must control.

Two examples: k in k-NN — minimised at k = 1 where it is exactly 0; λ in ridge/lasso — minimised at λ = 0, i.e. no regularization at all. So: use held-out data.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a) — training error hamesha kyun girta hai:

Model ki lachak barhane ka matlab hai ke ab wo zyada qism ke functions bana sakta hai. Aur jab options barh jaayein, to un mein se behtareen option pehle se bura nahi ho sakta — zyada se zyada barabar.

Misaal: aap ko 10 kitabon mein se behtareen chunni thi aur aap ne ek chuni. Ab main aap ko 100 kitabein de doon (purani 10 bhi shamil). Kya ab aap ki behtareen pasand pehle se buri ho sakti hai? Nahi — kam az kam wohi purani wali chun lenge. Isi liye training error girta jaata hai, aur aakhir mein 0 ho jaata hai.

Jawab (a) — test error kyun nahi girta rehta:

Test error mein do ulti cheezein ek saath chal rahi hoti hain:

  • Bias (model bohat saada hai, asal pattern pakad hi nahi sakta) — lachak barhne se ye girta hai. Acha.
  • Variance (model is data ka shor bhi pakad raha hai, jo naye data mein nahi hoga) — lachak barhne se ye barhta hai. Bura.

Shuru mein pehli cheez ghalib hoti hai to test error girta hai; ek had ke baad doosri ghalib ho jaati hai to test error barhne lagta hai. Isi liye U ki shakal banti hai, aur hamein us U ke sab se neeche wale point par rukna hai.

Jawab (b) — bay-kaar kyun: kyunki wo hamesha girta hai, to usay minimize karne se jawab hamesha "sab se zyada lachakdar model" aayega — yaani sab se zyada overfit. Us mein variance ke baare mein koi khabar hi nahi, jabke qaabu to usi par karna hai.

Jawab (c) — do misaalein:

  1. k-NN ka k: training error k = 1 par sab se kam hota hai, aur wahan wo bilkul 0 hota hai (har point apna hi parosi). Yaani training error hamesha sab se zyada overfit wala model chunega.
  2. ridge/lasso ka λ: training error λ = 0 par sab se kam hota hai — yaani jawab aayega "regularization bilkul mat lagao", jo poore maqsad ko khatam kar deta hai.

Yaad rakhein: ye masla polynomial degree aur tree depth par bhi laagu hai — training error hamesha sab se bada/gehra chunega. Is liye hyper-parameters hamesha held-out (validation) data se chunte hain.

Set 3 — 7 sawal

Features aur feature engineering

In sab ka mazmoon ek hai: model ko wo cheezein de rahe hain ya nahi, jin se wo asal pattern bana sake.

P-3.16 marks

A "city" feature is encoded Karachi = 1, Lahore = 2, Islamabad = 3, Peshawar = 4. What false claim does this impose, what is the correct encoding, and when would integer encoding be legitimate?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Kisi ne "city" wale column ko aise numbers de diye: Karachi = 1, Lahore = 2, Islamabad = 3, Peshawar = 4.

(a) Is se model par kaunsa jhoota daawa laad diya gaya?
(b) Theek tareeqa kya hai?
(c) Aise numbers dena kab jaayez hota hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

False claim: it imposes an order and a metric on an unordered category. The term is θcity × code, so the model is forced to assert the cities are ordered, that the spacing is equal and additive, and that Peshawar's effect is exactly four times Karachi's. The whole city effect is compressed into one coefficient along an ordering the analyst invented — and relabelling the cities would change the predictions, which proves the encoding is wrong.

Correct: one-hot — one binary indicator per city, so each gets its own free coefficient. (Drop one level if an offset is present.) With only 4 categories this is fine; the objections in B2 apply to large vocabularies.

When integers are legitimate: when categories are genuinely ordinal with roughly equal spacing — education level, a 1–5 rating. Also for trees, which only ask "is code ≤ 2?".

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a): is se model ko ye jhooti baat maanne par majboor kar diya gaya ke sheheron ki koi tarteeb hai aur un ke darmiyan faasla hai.

Kyun: model is column ko θcity × number ki shakal mein istemaal karta hai. Yaani wo samajhta hai:

  • Islamabad (3) Karachi (1) se "teen guna" hai
  • Lahore (2) Karachi aur Islamabad ke theek "beech" mein hai
  • Karachi se Lahore jaane ka asar bilkul utna hi hai jitna Islamabad se Peshawar jaane ka (dono ka farq 1 hai)

Ye sab bakwaas hai — sheheron ki koi tarteeb nahi hoti.

Sab se pakka saboot: agar hum numbers badal dein (Lahore = 1, Karachi = 2…) to model ki predictions badal jaayengi. Jis model ka jawab is par depend kare ke aap ne numbers kis tarteeb se diye the, wo model ghalat hai.

Jawab (b) — theek tareeqa: one-hot. Har sheher ko apna alag column dein, jis mein sirf 0 ya 1 ho:

Lahore wala ghar : Karachi Lahore Islamabad Peshawar 0 1 0 0

Ab har sheher ko apna azaad weight mil jaayega, aur koi jhooti tarteeb nahi. (Sirf 4 sheher hain to one-hot bilkul theek hai — B2 wale aitraaz hazaaron alfaaz par lagte hain, 4 par nahi.)

Jawab (c) — numbers kab jaayez hain: jab categories mein waqai tarteeb ho aur faasla taqreeban barabar ho. Misaal: taleem ka darja (primary=1, matric=2, BA=3, MA=4) ya 1–5 ki rating. Yahan "3 wala 1 wale se zyada parha hua hai" waqai sach hai. Aur decision trees ke liye bhi chal jaata hai, kyunki wo sirf poochte hain "number 2 se kam hai ya zyada?"

Yaad rakhein: khud se poochein — "kya in cheezon ko tarteeb mein lagaya ja sakta hai?" Haan (chhota/darmiyana/bara) → numbers chal jaayenge. Nahi (sheher, rang, brand) → one-hot.

P-3.26 marks

Crop yield rises with rainfall up to about 800 mm then falls from waterlogging. Construct a feature map letting a linear model capture this, show it produces the required shape, and state what property is preserved.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Fasal ki paidawar baarish ke saath barhti hai — magar sirf 800 mm tak. Us ke baad paani jama ho jaata hai aur paidawar girne lagti hai.

(a) Aisa feature map banayein jo ek linear model ko ye shakal pakadne de.
(b) Numbers se dikhayein ke shakal waqai banti hai.
(c) Kaunsi khoobi barqaraar rehti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The relationship is non-monotonic, which a single coefficient cannot express (one coefficient = one slope).

Hinge feature with a knot at 800:

phi(u) = ( 1 , u , max(0, u - 800) )

With θ2 = +0.05 and θ3 = −0.09:

u < 800 : slope = theta_2 = +0.05 (rising) u > 800 : slope = theta_2 + theta_3 = -0.04 (falling)

Peak at 800 mm, then decline — exactly as described. (A quadratic (1,u,u²) also works but forces a symmetric decline and places the peak wherever the fit likes.)

Property preserved: linear in θ — all non-linearity is inside the fixed map φ, so the risk stays convex and least squares applies unchanged.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle masla samjhein: paidawar pehle barhti hai, phir girti hai. Yaani do mukhtalif dhalaanein chahiye — ek oopar ki, ek neeche ki. Magar ek coefficient sirf ek dhalaan de sakta hai. Is liye saada model θ1 + θ2·baarish ye shakal bana hi nahi sakta.

Jawab (a) — hinge feature daalein, 800 par mod:

phi(u) = ( 1 , u , max(0, u - 800) )

Teesri feature kaise kaam karti hai: max(0, u − 800) ka matlab hai "agar baarish 800 se kam hai to 0, aur agar zyada hai to jitni zyada hai wo". Yaani ye feature 800 tak soyi rehti hai aur 800 ke baad jaag jaati hai. Isi liye 800 ke baad dhalaan badal jaati hai.

Jawab (b) — numbers se dekhein. Farz karein model ne seekha: θ2 = +0.05, θ3 = −0.09.

baarish 800 se KAM : teesri feature = 0, to dhalaan = +0.05 (BARHTI) baarish 800 se ZYADA: teesri feature chalu, dhalaan = 0.05 - 0.09 = -0.04 (GIRTI)

Bas — 800 tak barhi, phir girne lagi. Bilkul wohi shakal jo chahiye thi.

Jawab (c) — barqaraar khoobi: model abhi bhi θ mein linear hai.

Kyun ahem hai: hum ne model nahi badla, sirf features badli hain. Teesri feature bhi ek aam column hai (uska number nikaal kar table mein daal diya). Is liye wohi purana least squares bagair kisi tabdeeli ke chalta rahega, aur problem convex rahegi (ek hi gaddha, kai nahi).

Yaad rakhein: jab bhi rishta "pehle barhta phir girta" (ya ulta) ho → max(0, u − knot) wali hinge feature daalein. Aur knot wahan rakhein jahan mod hai (yahan 800).

P-3.36 marks

A says "the model is poor, so we need a neural network." B says "we need better features." The residuals show a clear systematic pattern against one input. Who is right, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Model bura chal raha hai. A kehta hai: "neural network lagao." B kehta hai: "behtar features banao." Aur ek saboot mojood hai: residuals mein ek input ke khilaaf saaf pattern dikh raha hai (yaani ghaltiyan be-tarteeb nahi, ek tarteeb se badal rahi hain).

Kaun theek hai, aur kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

B is right, on this evidence.

Why: for a well-specified model residuals should be random around zero. A systematic pattern against an input means the model cannot represent a relationship demonstrably present in the data — the true function is outside the hypothesis space. That is underfitting from an inadequate φ, not an inadequate model family. The residual plot has effectively told you which feature to add.

Why A is premature: a neural network is a non-convex, data-hungry, opaque way to discover what the residual plot already revealed; it adds local minima, hyper-parameters and variance, and discards interpretability for no gain.

When A would be right: if, after feature engineering, residuals stay structured in ways no hand-designed φ can capture — typically images, audio or raw text, with plenty of data.

Correct order: diagnose residuals → fix φ → regularize → only then a richer family.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: is saboot ki roshni mein B theek hai.

Kyun — residual ka pattern kya bata raha hai: agar model theek bana hua ho, to ghaltiyan be-tarteeb honi chahiye — kabhi oopar, kabhi neeche, bagair kisi qaide ke. Magar yahan tarteeb hai (jaise "jab input bara hota hai to ghalti hamesha oopar ki taraf hoti hai").

Is ka saaf matlab hai: data mein ek rishta mojood hai — residual plot usay dikha raha hai — magar model usay bayaan hi nahi kar sakta. Model ke paas wo shakal banane ki quwwat nahi. Isi ko underfitting kehte hain, aur iski wajah na-kaafi features hain.

Aur gaur karein: residual plot ne aap ko bata diya hai ke kaun si feature daalni hai (jis input ke khilaaf pattern dikha, usi ka square ya log ya hinge). Jab jawab saamne likha hua hai, to usay dhoondne ke liye neural network lagana bay-wajah hai.

A kyun jaldbaazi kar raha hai:

  • Neural network non-convex hai — kai gaddhe, aur jawab shuruati guess par depend karega.
  • Usay bohat zyada data chahiye.
  • Usay samajhna mushkil — aap bata nahi sakenge ke usne faisla kyun kiya.
  • Aur ye sab nuqsan utha kar bhi wo wohi cheez dhoondhega jo residual plot pehle hi dikha chuki hai.

A kab theek hoga: jab feature engineering ke baad bhi residuals mein aisi structure baaqi rahe jo haath se banayi features pakad na sakein — aam tor par tasveerein, awaaz ya raw text par, aur jab data bohat zyada ho.

Yaad rakhein — theek tarteeb: pehle residuals dekho → phir features theek karo → phir regularization lagao → aur phir kahin ja kar bada model socho.

P-3.46 marks

A sentiment classifier uses one-hot bag-of-words over 30,000 words with 8 classes, trained on 1,500 reviews. Compute the parameter count and the ratio to data size, state what will happen, and give two remedies.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek model reviews se andaza lagata hai ke banda khush tha ya naraz. Us ne 30,000 alfaaz ka one-hot istemaal kiya, 8 categories hain, aur training sirf 1,500 reviews par hui.

(a) Parameters ginein, aur data se nisbat nikaalein.
(b) Kya hoga?
(c) Do ilaaj batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

parameters = 30,000 x 8 = 240,000 ratio = 240,000 / 1,500 = 160 per example

What happens: severe overfitting. With d ≫ n the model can drive training error to zero by memorising which rare words appeared in which review. Many words occur once or twice, so their coefficients are fitted to pure noise. Training accuracy looks excellent, test accuracy is poor.

Remedy 1 — reduce d. Use a dense pre-trained embedding (50–300 dims), cutting parameters by ~2 orders of magnitude. It is also approximately faithful, so evidence is shared across synonyms instead of learned separately for each.

Remedy 2 — regularize heavily. Lasso is especially apt: most of the 30,000 words are irrelevant, so a sparsifying penalty zeroes them, giving feature selection and an interpretable word list.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a) — hisaab:

Har lafz ke liye, har category ka ek weight chahiye: parameters = 30,000 x 8 = 2,40,000 Aur misaalein sirf 1,500 hain: nisbat = 2,40,000 / 1,500 = 160

Yaani har ek misaal ke badle 160 knobs. Ye bilkul ulta hai — seekhne ke liye misaalein knobs se zyada honi chahiye, kam nahi.

Jawab (b) — kya hoga: shadeed overfitting (ratta).

Kyun: itne knobs ke saath model ko asal pattern samajhne ki zaroorat hi nahi. Wo ye kar sakta hai: "jis review mein lafz zabardast aaya tha wo khush tha, jis mein tanqeed aaya tha wo naraz tha" — aur 1,500 reviews hubahu yaad kar le. Aur kyunki bohat se alfaaz sirf ek ya do baar aaye hain, un ka weight ek-do misaal se seekha gaya — jo mehez ittefaq hai, koi pattern nahi.

Natija: training par shaandaar accuracy, naye review par bura haal.

Jawab (c) — do ilaaj:

1. Features kam karein (dense embedding). 30,000 ki jagah har lafz ko 50–300 numbers ka chhota vector dein (word2vec / GloVe). Parameters taqreeban 100 guna kam ho jaate hain. Aur ek bara faida: aise embedding mein hum-maani alfaaz paas paas hote hain, to "zabardast" se seekha hua sabaq "shaandaar" par bhi laagu ho jaata hai — yaani 1,500 misaalein zyada door tak chalti hain.

2. Sakht regularization (lasso). 30,000 alfaaz mein se zyadatar (the, is, aur, main…) mood ke baare mein kuch nahi batate. Lasso un ka weight bilkul zero kar deta hai, to bache hue alfaaz ki ek chhoti, samajh mein aane wali list mil jaati hai. (Ridge sab ko chhota karega magar zero nahi, to shor baaqi rahega.)

Yaad rakhein: jab bhi "features ki ginti" aur "misaalon ki ginti" sawal mein saath aayein — un ki nisbat nikaalein. Agar features zyada nikal aayein, to jawab hai: overfitting + (features kam karein ya regularize karein).

P-3.56 marks

Define faithful embedding, then judge whether each encoding of {small, medium, large} is faithful: (a) one-hot; (b) 1, 2, 3; (c) every category mapped to 1.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Pehle "faithful embedding" ki tareef dein. Phir {small, medium, large} ke teen tareeqon par faisla dein ke kaunsa faithful hai:

(a) One-hot (teen alag columns)
(b) small = 1, medium = 2, large = 3
(c) teeno ko 1 bana dena

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Definition. φ is faithful if it preserves the raw data's meaningful distinctions and relationships: (1) meaningfully different items → different vectors, and (2) the geometry reflects real similarity.

(a) One-hot — faithful enough, the safe default. Satisfies (1) fully. Fails (2) in principle (all pairs √2 apart, so it does not encode that medium lies between), but each category gets a free coefficient and with 3 categories the dimensionality objection does not arise.

(b) 1, 2, 3 — faithful here, because these categories are genuinely ordinal. The geometry actually helps: medium sits between small and large, matching reality, using one parameter instead of three. Caveat: it also asserts equal spacing and a monotone effect.

(c) All → 1 — definitely not faithful. Violates (1) outright: three distinguishable inputs collapse to one vector, so information is irrecoverably destroyed and the model can never use size at all.

Lesson: faithfulness depends on the encoding together with the structure of the raw data.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareef: "faithful" ka matlab hai wafaadar, imaandar. Ek embedding faithful hai agar wo raw data ki asal baat imaandari se numbers mein utaar de. Do sharten:

  1. Jo cheezein waqai alag hain, wo alag numbers par jaayein (information zaya na ho).
  2. Jo cheezein waqai milti-julti hain, wo numbers mein bhi paas paas hon.

(a) One-hot — kaafi had tak faithful. Mehfooz chunaav.

Kyun: pehli shart poori — teeno ko alag vector mila, kuch zaya nahi hua. Doosri shart usoolan poori nahi, kyunki one-hot mein teeno barabar door hote hain — wo ye nahi batata ke "medium, small aur large ke beech mein hai". Magar faida ye hai ke har category ko apna azaad weight milta hai, aur sirf 3 categories par dimension ka koi masla nahi.

(b) 1, 2, 3 — yahan faithful hai.

Kyun: ye P-3.1 wale sheheron se bilkul mukhtalif soorat hai! Sheheron ki koi tarteeb nahi thi, magar chhota < darmiyana < bara — ye tarteeb waqai mojood hai. To yahan geometry jhoot nahi bol rahi, sach bol rahi hai: medium waqai beech mein hai. Aur faida ye ke teen ke bajaye ek hi weight se kaam chal jaata hai (kam data mein ye bara faida hai).

Magar ehtiyaat: ye ye bhi maan leta hai ke faasla barabar hai (small→medium ka asar = medium→large ka asar). Agar haqeeqat mein large ka jump bohat bara ho, to ye jhoot ban jaayega — phir one-hot behtar.

(c) Teeno ko 1 — bilkul faithful nahi.

Kyun: pehli shart seedha tootti hai. Teen alag cheezein ek hi number par jama ho gayin, yaani information hamesha ke liye mar gayi. Ab wo column har row mein 1 hai — yaani ek constant. Model chahe kitna bhi data le le, wo "size" ka faida kabhi utha hi nahi sakega, kyunki us tak size ki khabar pohonch hi nahi rahi.

Yaad rakhein — asal sabaq: faithful hona encoding ki akeli khoobi nahi. Wohi 1,2,3 sheheron ke liye ghalat tha aur sizes ke liye theek hai. Faisla is baat par hai ke asal cheez mein tarteeb mojood hai ya nahi.

P-3.66 marks

Why is standardization essential for k-NN and for ridge, but irrelevant to the predictions of unregularized least squares? Illustrate the ridge case.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Standardization (har feature ko ek jaise paimane par le aana) k-NN aur ridge ke liye lazmi hai — magar bagair-regularization wale least squares ki predictions par is ka koi asar nahi. Teeno ke liye wajah batayein, aur ridge wala case numbers se samjhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

k-NN — essential. There are no parameters; everything is decided by distance, so the widest-range feature dominates and nothing inside the model can compensate. Predictions also become unit-dependent.

Ridge — essential, because the penalty hits coefficients, whose size depends on the feature's units:

Same real effect, +3000 rupees per m^2 : in m^2 : theta = 3000 -> penalty 3000^2 = 9,000,000 in ft^2 : theta = 278.7 -> penalty 278.7^2 = 77,674 ratio = 115.9

The identical relationship is penalised 116× more heavily just because of the unit, so the fit depends on an arbitrary choice. Standardizing makes λ penalise all features comparably.

Plain least squares — no effect. Rescaling feature j by c just rescales θj by 1/c, leaving every product θjxj — and so every prediction — unchanged.

One line: scaling matters wherever features are compared — in a distance or in a penalty.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. k-NN — lazmi hai.

Kyun: k-NN mein koi parameter hi nahi hota. Sab kuch faasle se tay hota hai. To jis feature ki range sab se badi hai (jaise tankhwah: hazaaron mein), wo faasle par qabza kar leta hai, aur chhoti range wala feature (jaise bachon ki ginti: 0–5) nazar hi nahi aata. Aur model ke andar koi aisi cheez nahi jo isay theek kar sake.

2. Ridge — lazmi hai.

Kyun: ridge ka jurmana coefficients par lagta hai. Aur coefficient ka size is baat par depend karta hai ke feature kis unit mein hai. Dekhein:

Bilkul WOHI asal rishta: 3000 rupaye har murabba meter Meter mein likhein: theta = 3000 -> jurmana 3000^2 = 90,00,000 Foot mein likhein: theta = 278.7 -> jurmana 278.7^2 = 77,674 Farq = 90,00,000 / 77,674 = 116 guna

Yaani ek hi haqeeqat ko sirf unit badalne par 116 guna zyada saza mil rahi hai! To ridge meter wale version ko bohat zyada dabaa dega, aur model ka jawab is bay-maani baat par depend karega ke aap ne meter likha tha ya foot. Standardize karne se saare features ek paimane par aa jaate hain, aur λ sab ko insaaf se saza deta hai.

3. Saada least squares — koi asar nahi.

Kyun: yahan koi jurmana nahi hai, to model ko azaadi hai. Agar aap feature ko 10 guna bara kar dein, to model khud uska coefficient 10 guna chhota kar lega. Dono ka zarb (θj × xj) wohi reh jaata hai, is liye prediction bilkul nahi badalti. (Haan, hisaab ki raftaar behtar ho jaati hai, magar jawab wohi.)

Yaad rakhein — ek line: scaling wahan zaroori hai jahan features ka ek doosre se moazna hota hai — yaani kisi faasle mein (k-NN) ya kisi jurmane mein (ridge/lasso). Jahan har feature ka coefficient apni unit khud sambhal leta hai, wahan zaroori nahi.

P-3.76 marks

An airfare model uses days_before_departure and is_weekend as separate additive features. Booking late is far more severe for weekend flights. What is missing, write the corrected map, and show numerically that it fixes it.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Hawai ticket ki keemat ka model do features use karta hai: kitne din pehle booking aur weekend hai ya nahi — magar dono alag alag, jama kar ke. Masla ye hai ke weekend ki flights par late booking ka nuqsan bohat zyada hota hai.

(a) Kya cheez missing hai?
(b) Theek feature map likhein.
(c) Numbers se dikhayein ke masla hal ho gaya.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Missing: an interaction feature. An additive model gives θ2 as the day-effect whatever the weekend flag, and θ3 merely shifts the line by a constant. It cannot express "the day-effect is stronger at weekends", which needs the slope in days to depend on the flag.

phi(u) = ( 1 , days , weekend , days x weekend )

With θ = (200, −2, 50, −3):

slope in days, weekday = theta_2 = -2 per day slope in days, weekend = theta_2 + theta_4 = -5 per day booking 25 days later: weekday +50 weekend +125 (2.5x worse)

The interaction supplies a second, independent slope. Without it one θ2 is fitted to a compromise, systematically under-predicting weekend late bookings — a systematic residual pattern, the signature of underfitting.

Note: still linear in θ. If many interactions are plausible, generate them and let lasso select.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a): interaction feature missing hai.

Kyun — "additive" ka masla: jab model aise hota hai

keemat = 200 + (-2)(din) + 50(weekend)

to −2 ka matlab hai: "ek din aage booking karne ka asar −2 hai" — aur ye hamesha −2 hai, chahe weekend ho ya na ho. Aur +50 ka matlab sirf itna hai ke weekend ki poori line 50 oopar khisak jaati hai. Yaani dono lines parallel rehti hain.

Magar sawal keh raha hai ke weekend par din ka asar zyada hai — yaani lines parallel nahi, un ki dhalaan alag honi chahiye. Additive model ye bayaan hi nahi kar sakta.

Jawab (b) — dono ka zarb (product) daal dein:

phi(u) = ( 1 , days , weekend , days x weekend )

Chauthi feature ka kamaal: weekday par weekend = 0, to days × 0 = 0 — wo feature soyi rehti hai. Weekend par weekend = 1, to wo jaag jaati hai aur dhalaan badal deti hai.

Jawab (c) — numbers se. Farz karein θ = (200, −2, 50, −3):

WEEKDAY par din ki dhalaan = -2 = -2 per din WEEKEND par din ki dhalaan = -2 + (-3) = -5 per din 25 din baad booking karein to keemat barhti hai: weekday : +50 weekend : +125 -> 2.5 guna zyada (bilkul wohi shikayat)

Dekha — ab weekend ki dhalaan apni alag ho gayi, aur wo data se khud seekhi gayi (θ4). Pehle ek hi −2 dono ka "beech ka samjhauta" ban gaya tha, is liye weekend ki late bookings ko ba-qaida kam bata raha tha.

Yaad rakhein: jab sawal kahe "X ka asar Y par depend karta hai" (ya "jab dono ek saath hon to masla hai") → interaction feature = X × Y daalein. Aur model θ mein linear hi rehta hai, to least squares waise hi chalega.

Set 4 — 5 sawal

Nearest neighbour predictors

Yaad rahe: NN kuch "seekhta" nahi — wo saara data yaad kar leta hai aur faasle se faisla karta hai.

P-4.16 marks

A soft-NN predictor has three stored points at squared distances 0, 2 and 8 from a query, with outcomes y = (10, 20, 90). Compute the prediction for β = 2 and β = 50, and interpret.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Soft-NN ke paas teen purane points hain. Naye point se un ke squared faasle hain: 0, 2, 8 (yaani pehla bilkul upar, teesra sab se door). Un ke jawab hain: 10, 20, 90.

β = 2 aur β = 50 — dono ke liye prediction nikaalein, aur batayein ke farq se kya samajh aata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

beta = 2 : scores 1.000 0.3679 0.0183 sum = 1.3862 weights 0.7214 0.2654 0.0132 y-hat = 0.7214(10) + 0.2654(20) + 0.0132(90) = 13.71 beta = 50 : scores 1.000 0.9608 0.8521 sum = 2.8129 weights 0.3555 0.3416 0.3029 y-hat = 0.3555(10) + 0.3416(20) + 0.3029(90) = 37.65 reference : 1-NN = 10 plain average = 40

Interpretation. At β = 2 the far point (y = 90) gets weight 0.013 and is almost ignored, so the answer sits near the 1-NN value of 10 — strict reliance on the closest case, high variance, overfitting risk.

At β = 50 the weights are nearly equal, the far point now contributes more than the other two combined, and the answer sits near the plain average of 40 — similarity has stopped mattering, underfitting risk.

So β interpolates between 1-NN and the constant mean. Note how much it changes one prediction (13.71 vs 37.65 on identical data) — which is why it must be chosen by validation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareeqa: har point ka "score" nikaalein exp(−faasla/β) se, phir unko jama kar ke taqseem kar dein (taake saare weights jama kar ke 1 ban jaayein).

--- beta = 2 --- score 1 = exp(-0/2) = exp(0) = 1.0000 score 2 = exp(-2/2) = exp(-1) = 0.3679 score 3 = exp(-8/2) = exp(-4) = 0.0183 jama = 1.3862 weight 1 = 1.0000/1.3862 = 0.7214 weight 2 = 0.3679/1.3862 = 0.2654 weight 3 = 0.0183/1.3862 = 0.0132 y-hat = 0.7214(10) + 0.2654(20) + 0.0132(90) = 13.71 --- beta = 50 --- score 1 = exp(0) = 1.0000 score 2 = exp(-0.04) = 0.9608 score 3 = exp(-0.16) = 0.8521 jama = 2.8129 weight 1 = 0.3555 weight 2 = 0.3416 weight 3 = 0.3029 y-hat = 0.3555(10) + 0.3416(20) + 0.3029(90) = 37.65

Ab matlab samjhein. Do cheezein moazne ke liye: agar 1-NN hota to jawab 10 aata (sirf sab se qareeb point). Agar saada average lete to 40 aata.

β = 2 par jawab 13.71 — yaani 10 ke bohat qareeb. Kyun? Dekhein door wale point (y = 90) ka weight sirf 0.0132 hai — taqreeban kuch bhi nahi. Model usay nazar-andaaz kar raha hai. Yaani model sirf "sab se milte julte case" par bharosa kar raha hai — 1-NN jaisa, aur overfitting ka khatra.

β = 50 par jawab 37.65 — yaani 40 ke qareeb. Kyun? Ab teeno weights taqreeban barabar hain (0.36, 0.34, 0.30). Door wala point ab 0.3029 × 90 = 27.3 daal raha hai — jo baqi dono se zyada hai! Yaani faasle ka faida khatam ho gaya, model taqreeban saada average de raha hai — underfitting.

Yaad rakhein: β ek knob hai jo model ko 1-NN aur saade average ke darmiyan ghumata hai. Aur dekhein kitna bara farq hai — wohi data, jawab 13.71 ya 37.65. Isi liye β ko andaze se nahi, validation se chunna lazmi hai.

P-4.26 marks

Show that soft-NN approaches 1-NN as β → 0 and a constant predictor as β → ∞. Which extreme is overfitting, and name another hyper-parameter that behaves the same way.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Saabit karein ke:

(a) β bohat chhota karne par soft-NN 1-NN ban jaata hai.
(b) β bohat bara karne par wo constant predictor (sirf average) ban jaata hai.
(c) Dono siron mein se kaunsa overfitting hai?
(d) Ek aur hyper-parameter batayein jo isi tarah behave karta hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

β → 0. Factor out the smallest distance dmin; the common factor cancels, so each weight depends on exp(−(di − dmin)/β). For the nearest point the exponent is 0 → 1; for all others di − dmin > 0, so the factor → 0. Hence wmin → 1 and ŷ → ymin — the 1-NN predictor.

β → ∞. Every exponent → 0, so every score → 1, the denominator → n, and every weight → 1/n. So ŷ → (1/n)Σyi — the mean, independent of x.

Which is which: β → 0 (1-NN) is overfitting — max flexibility, zero training error, max variance. β → ∞ is underfitting — high bias, ignores x.

Others: k in k-NN (direction reversed: k=1 overfits, k=n gives the mean), λ in ridge/lasso (λ=0 overfits, λ→∞ leaves only the offset), and polynomial degree. All chosen by validation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) β bohat chhota → 1-NN

Aasaan tareeqa samajhne ka: P-4.1 ka hisaab dobara dekhein. β = 2 par door wale point ka weight pehle hi sirf 0.0132 reh gaya tha. Ab β ko 0.1 kar dein — exponent −8/0.1 = −80 ho jaayega, aur exp(−80) taqreeban sifar hai. Yaani door wale points ka weight bilkul mar jaata hai aur poora weight sab se qareeb point par aa jaata hai. Jab ek hi point ka weight 1 ho aur baqi ka 0, to ye 1-NN hi hai.

Formal daleel: sab se chhote faasle dmin ko bahar nikaal dein; wo numerator aur denominator se kat jaata hai, to har weight exp(−(di − dmin)/β) par reh jaata hai. Qareeb wale point ke liye ye exponent 0 hai (yaani weight 1), baqi sab ke liye musbat hai (yaani β → 0 par weight 0).

(b) β bohat bara → constant (mean)

Kyun: β bohat bara ho to faasla/β taqreeban 0 ho jaata hai — chahe faasla 2 ho ya 8. Aur exp(0) = 1. Yaani sab ke score 1 ho gaye, sab ke weight barabar (1/n), aur jawab sab ka saada average ban gaya. Ghaur karein: ye jawab x par depend hi nahi karta — naya point kahin bhi ho, jawab wohi average. Yehi constant predictor hai.

(c) Kaunsa sira kya hai:

  • β → 0 (1-NN) = overfitting — lachak maximum, training error zero, variance maximum.
  • β → ∞ (mean) = underfitting — model x ko dekhta hi nahi.

(d) Isi tarah ke doosre hyper-parameters:

  • k-NN ka k — magar taraf ulti hai: k = 1 overfit, aur k = n par mean.
  • ridge/lasso ka λ — λ = 0 overfit, aur λ bohat bara ho to θ → 0, sirf offset bachta hai (phir bhi constant predictor!).
  • polynomial degree — degree 0 constant, bohat badi degree overfit.

Yaad rakhein: ye chaaron knobs asal mein ek hi U-curve par safar karte hain — ek sira overfit, doosra underfit, aur sahi jagah beech mein. Aur chaaron validation se chune jaate hain.

P-4.36 marks

A fraud team uses 1-NN where 99.8% of transactions are legitimate, and reports 99.8% test accuracy. Why is this misleading, and what metric and predictor change do you recommend?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek fraud team 1-NN use kar rahi hai. Data mein 99.8% transactions theek hain (sirf 0.2% fraud). Team fakhr se batati hai: "hamari test accuracy 99.8% hai!"

(a) Ye number kyun dhoka hai?
(b) Kaunsa metric use karna chahiye?
(c) Predictor mein kya tabdeeli karein?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Why misleading: the baseline is already 99.8%. A predictor that ignores its input and always says "legitimate" scores 99.8% — that is the constant predictor, and it catches zero fraud. So the model has shown no skill; the number comes entirely from class imbalance. Accuracy also weights both error types equally, though a missed fraud costs far more.

Metrics: recall on the fraud class (the business objective), precision (the investigation workload), the precision–recall curve, or best, an explicit cost/utility metric.

Predictor changes: output a score, not a label — use soft-NN, whose weighted average gives a continuous fraud score so the threshold can be tuned to capacity. Raise k (but use the weighted proportion of fraudulent neighbours, since majority voting always says "legitimate" here). Standardize features. Consider reframing as anomaly detection.

Principle: always compare against the trivial constant-predictor baseline.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a): kyunki ek bilkul bewaqoof model bhi 99.8% le aayega.

Soch kar dekhein: main ek "model" banata hoon jo input dekhta hi nahi — bas har transaction par likh deta hai "theek hai". Uski accuracy kya hogi? 99.8% — kyunki waqai 99.8% theek hi hain! Magar is model ne ek bhi fraud nahi pakda. Bilkul bay-kaar hai.

To 99.8% ka matlab ye nahi ke model acha hai — ye number poora class imbalance se aaya hai (ek qism bohat zyada, doosri bohat kam). Team ne asal mein kuch bhi saabit nahi kiya.

Doosra masla: accuracy dono ghaltiyon ko barabar samajhti hai. Magar haqeeqat mein ek fraud chhoot jaana lakhon ka nuqsan hai, aur ek theek transaction ko ghalti se check kar lena sirf 5 minute ka kaam.

Jawab (b) — kaunsa metric:

  • Recall (fraud class par) — "jitne fraud hue the, un mein se kitne pakde?" Yehi asal maqsad hai.
  • Precision — "jitne flag kiye, un mein se kitne waqai fraud nikle?" Isi se check karne walon ka bojh pata chalta hai.
  • Ya sab se behtar: cost metric — ek chhoota fraud kitne ka nuqsan, ek ghalat alarm kitne ka. Phir poora hisaab paison mein.

Jawab (c) — predictor mein tabdeeli:

  1. Label ki jagah score dein. 1-NN "haan/nahi" deta hai. Soft-NN use karein — uska weighted average ek number deta hai (0 se 1 ke darmiyan). Phir team apni quwwat ke hisaab se had tay kar sakti hai: "sab se mushtaba 500 roz dekho".
  2. k barhayein — magar ehtiyaat: majority vote yahan kaam nahi karega (10 parosiyon mein se 10 hi theek honge, to jawab hamesha "theek" aayega). Is liye fraud parosiyon ka hissa (percentage) score banayein.
  3. Features standardize karein — raqam hazaaron mein hai, transactions ki ginti ikaayiyon mein.
  4. Aur ghaur karein ke kya anomaly detection behtar framing hai — kyunki naye qism ka fraud purane data mein mojood hi nahi hoga, to uska "qareebi parosi" koi nahi milega.

Yaad rakhein — aam usool: koi bhi accuracy number dekhein, to pehle poochein: "bewaqoof model kitna le aata?" Us se behtar nahi hai to model ne kuch nahi seekha. (Regression mein wohi baat variance ki hoti hai.)

P-4.46 marks

Compare 1-NN, k-NN and soft-NN on how the prediction is formed, hyper-parameters, and sensitivity. Give one situation where soft-NN is clearly preferable to k-NN.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Teeno ka moazna karein: 1-NN, k-NN, soft-NN — (1) jawab kaise banta hai, (2) kaunsa knob hai, (3) sensitivity (yaani input zara badlein to jawab kitna badalta hai). Aur ek aisi soorat batayein jahan soft-NN saaf tor par k-NN se behtar hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

1-NNk-NNSoft NN
PredictionCopy the closest yMean/vote of k closest; others weight exactly 0Σwiyi over all points
Hyper-paramNonek — discreteβ — continuous
SensitivityMax — step functionLower but still jumpsLowest & continuous

Where soft-NN clearly wins: when a smooth output is required. For a robot joint angle or a steering setpoint, k-NN gives a piecewise-constant output that holds still and then jumps the moment the k-neighbour set changes membership. In a control system those jumps appear as jerks and can excite oscillation. Soft-NN's weights vary continuously, so the output is smooth.

Where k-NN wins: prediction cost — only k neighbours via a spatial index, versus all n exponentials per query.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1-NNk-NNSoft NN
Jawab kaiseSab se qareeb ka jawab copyk qareebi ka average; baqi ka hissa bilkul 0Saare points ka weighted average
KnobKoi nahik — tukdon mein (1, 2, 3…)β — musalsal (koi bhi number)
SensitivitySab se zyada — chhalaang maarta haiKam, magar phir bhi chhalaangSab se kam — narm, chhalaang nahi

k-NN mein chhalaang kyun aati hai: farz karein k = 5. Aap input ko dheere dheere khiska rahe hain. Kuch der tak wohi 5 parosi rehte hain, to jawab bilkul nahi badalta. Phir achaanak ek naya point andar aa jaata hai aur purana bahar — aur jawab ek dam chhalaang laga deta hai. Yaani jawab tukdon mein pakka rehta hai aur phir kood jaata hai.

Soft-NN kab saaf behtar hai: jab jawab narm (smooth) chahiye.

Misaal: robot ke haath ka angle, ya gaadi ka steering. Yahan chhalaang ka matlab hai jhatka — machine hilegi, aur buri soorat mein kaanpne lagegi. Soft-NN mein saare weights dheere dheere badalte hain (kyunki koi point bilkul bahar nahi hota), to jawab bhi narmi se badalta hai. Control systems mein ye lazmi hai.

Do aur wajuhat: (1) jab data kahin ghana aur kahin khaali ho, to pakka k ghane ilaaqe mein bohat chhota halqa aur khaali ilaaqe mein bohat bara halqa bana deta hai — jabke β ek pakki lambai tay karta hai. (2) β ko baareeki se tune kar sakte hain; k sirf 3 ya 4 ho sakta hai, 3.5 nahi.

k-NN kahan behtar: raftaar. k-NN ko sirf k parosi dhoondne hain (aur is ke liye tez tareeqe mojood hain), jabke soft-NN ko har sawal par saare n points ka hisaab karna padta hai.

P-4.56 marks

Why is nearest-neighbour called "instance-based" in the lectures' taxonomy, and what distinguishes it from a linear predictor in what is learned, what is stored, and when computation happens?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Lecture nearest-neighbour ko "instance-based" kehta hai (yaani "misaalon par mabni"). Kyun? Aur linear predictor se uska farq kya hai in teen baaton mein:

(a) Seekha kya jaata hai?
(b) Mehfooz kya hota hai?
(c) Hisaab kab hota hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

It is instance-based because the model is the stored data — predictions come from direct comparison against retained instances, not from evaluating a fitted functional form.

Nearest neighbourLinear predictor
LearnedNothing fitted — no θ, no ERM; only k/β selectedθ fitted by (regularized) ERM
StoredAll n data pairsOnly d numbers — data can be discarded
ComputationAt prediction — O(nd) per query. "Lazy"At training — one dot product per query. "Eager"

Two consequences. (1) Since nothing is fitted there is nothing to regularize — no θ whose size could be penalised — so flexibility must be controlled through the distance function and neighbourhood size. That is why standardization is mandatory for NN: the distance is the model.

(2) NN adapts to any function shape without specifying φ, but degrades badly in high dimensions as distances concentrate. The trade is that NN moves the effort from choosing φ to choosing a metric.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Naam kyun "instance-based": kyunki model asal mein wohi mehfooz data hai. Yahan koi formula fit nahi hota — jawab purani misaalon se seedha moazna kar ke milta hai. Yaani data hi model hai.

Farq teen baaton mein:

Nearest neighbourLinear predictor
(a) Seekha kyaKuch bhi fit nahi hota — na θ, na ERM. Sirf k ya β chuna jaata haiθ (weights) ERM se fit hote hain
(b) Mehfooz kyaSaara data — saare n points. Memory data ke saath barhti haiSirf d numbers — data ko phenk sakte hain
(c) Hisaab kabJawab dene ke waqt — har sawal par saare points se faasla naapna. Isay "lazy" (aalsi) kehte hainTraining ke waqt — phir har jawab sirf ek zarb-jama. Isay "eager" kehte hain

Do ahem nataij:

1. Regularize karne ko kuch nahi hai. Regularization ka matlab hai θ ko chhota rakhna. Magar NN mein θ hi nahi! To lachak ko qaabu karne ka ek hi rasta hai: faasla kaise naapa jaaye aur kitne parosi lein. Isi liye NN mein standardization lazmi hai — kyunki faasla hi model hai, aur faasla kharab hua to model kharab.

2. Zyada dimensions mein NN bura ho jaata hai. NN ka faida ye hai ke usay φ batane ki zaroorat nahi, wo kisi bhi shakal ke saath dhal jaata hai. Magar jab features bohat zyada hon (jaise 100), to saare faasle ek jaise lagne lagte hain aur "sab se qareeb" point waqai qareeb hi nahi hota. Yaani NN ne mehnat φ chunne se hata kar faasla chunne par daal di — aur zyada dimensions mein wo mehnat zyada mushkil ho jaati hai.

Yaad rakhein: NN = "data hi model hai". Is se teen baatein khud-ba-khud nikalti hain: training muft, prediction mehngi, memory bohat; aur regularization ki jagah standardization.

Set 5 — 8 sawal

Losses, constant predictors aur hisaab

Kuch sawal hisaab maangte hain — haath se practise karein, calculator ke bagair. Asal baat yaad rahe: loss badalne se jawab badal jaata hai.

P-5.16 marks

For y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) compute the optimal constant and the empirical risk under (a) square loss, (b) absolute loss. (c) Verify each beats the other under its own loss. (d) Replace 24 by 10 and comment.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Data: y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24). (Ghaur karein — 24 baqi sab se bohat alag hai, yaani outlier.)

(a) Square loss ke saath behtareen akela number (constant) aur uska error nikaalein.
(b) Wohi absolute loss ke saath.
(c) Tasdeeq karein ke har ek apne loss par doosre se behtar hai.
(d) 24 ki jagah 10 rakh kar dekhein kya badla.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) SQUARE -> MEAN = 50/5 = 10 MSE = (49+9+9+1+196)/5 = 264/5 = 52.8 (= variance) (b) ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN. n=5 odd, median = y_3 = 7 check n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 ok n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 ok MAE = (4+0+0+2+17)/5 = 23/5 = 4.6 (c) CROSS-CHECK MAE at the mean (10) = (7+3+3+1+14)/5 = 28/5 = 5.6 > 4.6 ok MSE at the median (7) = (16+0+0+4+289)/5 = 309/5 = 61.8 > 52.8 ok (d) 24 -> 10 : y = (3,7,7,9,10) new mean = 36/5 = 7.2 (was 10.0) moved 2.8 new median = 7 (was 7.0) moved 0.0

Comment on (d): changing one value by 14 moved the mean by 2.8 (exactly 14/5, since every value enters with weight 1/n) and the median not at all. That is robustness quantified.

Why: the derivative of MAE is n1/n − n2/n — it depends only on how many points lie each side, never how far. So the outlier's magnitude is irrelevant to the median; the mean sums values directly and feels every one.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Square loss ka jawab = MEAN (average)

mean = (3 + 7 + 7 + 9 + 24) / 5 = 50/5 = 10 Error (MSE) nikaalein — har point se farq ka square: (10-3)^2 = 49 (10-7)^2 = 9 (10-7)^2 = 9 (10-9)^2 = 1 (10-24)^2 = 196 jama = 264 MSE = 264/5 = 52.8 (aur yehi data ka variance hai)

(b) Absolute loss ka jawab = MEDIAN (beech wala)

5 points hain (taaq), to median teesra number = 7 Tasdeeq: 7 se chhote kitne? sirf 3 -> 1/5 = 0.2 (0.5 se kam, theek) 7 se bare kitne? 9 aur 24 -> 2/5 = 0.4 (0.5 se kam, theek) Error (MAE) — har point se seedha farq: |7-3| = 4 |7-7| = 0 |7-7| = 0 |7-9| = 2 |7-24| = 17 jama = 23 MAE = 23/5 = 4.6

(c) Cross-check — har loss ka apna champion

MEAN (10) par MAE nikaalein = (7+3+3+1+14)/5 = 5.6 vs median par 4.6 -> absolute loss ke liye MEDIAN jeeta MEDIAN (7) par MSE nikaalein = (16+0+0+4+289)/5 = 61.8 vs mean par 52.8 -> square loss ke liye MEAN jeeta

Yaani koi ek "sahi average" nahi hota — jawab is par depend karta hai ke aap kaunsa loss use kar rahe hain.

(d) Outlier hata kar dekhein

24 ki jagah 10 rakh dein: y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 10) naya mean = 36/5 = 7.2 (pehle 10.0 tha) -> 2.8 khisak gaya naya median = 7 (pehle 7.0 tha) -> 0.0 bilkul nahi hila

Ye sab se ahem nukta hai. Ek value 14 badalne se mean 2.8 khisak gaya magar median bilkul nahi hila. Isi ko "robustness" kehte hain.

Kyun aisa hota hai:

  • Mean ke formula mein har value seedha jama hoti hai. To 24 ko 10 karne se jama 14 kam hua, aur 5 se taqseem kar ke 14/5 = 2.8 khisak gaya. Mean ko har value mehsoos hoti hai.
  • Median sirf ye dekhta hai ke kitne points is ke oopar hain aur kitne neeche. 24 ho ya 10 — dono soorton mein wo 7 ke oopar hi hai, ginti wohi rahi. Is liye median ko farq hi nahi pada. Outlier ki value us ke liye bay-maani hai — chahe 24 ho ya 2400.

Yaad rakhein: outlier hon to median / absolute loss; saaf data ho to mean / square loss. Aur MSE apne optimum par data ka variance hota hai — ye baat likhne par extra mark milta hai.

P-5.26 marks

For y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) find the 0.75- and 0.25-quantiles using the optimality condition. Which τ gives each, and describe a delivery-time scenario for each.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Isi data y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) ke liye 0.75-quantile aur 0.25-quantile nikaalein (shart laga kar, andaze se nahi). Phir batayein kaunsa τ kaunsa jawab deta hai, aur delivery-time ki ek ek misaal dein.

(Shart: θ τ-quantile hai agar n1/n ≤ τ ≤ 1 − n2/n, jahan n1 = kitne points chhote, n2 = kitne bade.)

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

0.75-QUANTILE. theta = 9: n_1 = #{y<9} = 3 -> 0.6 n_2 = #{y>9} = 1 -> 0.2 range = [0.6 , 0.8] 0.75 is inside ok => answer 9 0.25-QUANTILE. theta = 7: n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 range = [0.2 , 0.6] 0.25 is inside ok => answer 7 Note: theta = 7 covers tau from 0.2 to 0.6, so it is simultaneously the 0.25-quantile AND the median. With only 5 points quantiles are coarse.

τ = 0.75 → quote 9 minutes. τ > ½ penalises under-estimation (lateness) more, so this is the customer promise: a cautious quote, only ~25% arriving later than promised.

τ = 0.25 → quote 7 minutes. τ < ½ penalises over-estimation more — an internal use: telling a rider when the order is ready, where over-estimating wastes paid idle time.

Key point: τ is a business decision, not estimated from data. Two teams can fit different τ to the same dataset.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareeqa: koi number try karein, uske oopar aur neeche ke points ginein, aur range bana lein.

--- 0.75-quantile: theta = 9 try karein --- 9 se chhote kitne? 3, 7, 7 -> n_1 = 3 -> 3/5 = 0.6 9 se bare kitne? 24 -> n_2 = 1 -> 1/5 = 0.2 range = [ 0.6 , 1 - 0.2 ] = [ 0.6 , 0.8 ] 0.75 is range mein hai? HAAN => 0.75-quantile = 9 --- 0.25-quantile: theta = 7 try karein --- 7 se chhote kitne? 3 -> n_1 = 1 -> 0.2 7 se bare kitne? 9, 24 -> n_2 = 2 -> 0.4 range = [ 0.2 , 1 - 0.4 ] = [ 0.2 , 0.6 ] 0.25 is range mein hai? HAAN => 0.25-quantile = 7

Ek dilchasp baat: θ = 7 ki range 0.2 se 0.6 tak hai — yaani wo ek hi waqt mein 0.25-quantile aur median (0.5) dono hai! Wajah: sirf 5 points hain. Quantiles ke liye bohat data chahiye hota hai, warna wo motay tukdon mein aate hain.

Delivery-time ki misaalein:

τ = 0.75 → customer ko 9 minute batayein.

Kyun: τ aadhe se zyada hai, to ye kam batane (late hone) par zyada saza lagata hai. Natija: model ehtiyaat se lamba waqt batata hai. Customer se wada karne ke liye yehi theek hai — taqreeban sirf 25% deliveries wade se der se aayengi.

τ = 0.25 → 7 minute.

Kyun: τ aadhe se kam hai, to ye zyada batane par zyada saza lagata hai. Ye andar ke kaam ke liye theek hai — jaise rider ko batana ke order kab tayyar hoga. Agar aap usay zyada waqt bata dein to wo khaali bethega aur uski tankhwah zaya hogi; kam bata dein to wo thoda pehle aa jaayega, koi bari baat nahi. Ulta hisaab, ulta τ.

Yaad rakhein: τ data se nahi nikalta — ye ek business faisla hai. Ek hi data par company ke do mukhtalif departments jaayez tor par mukhtalif τ rakh sakte hain, kyunki un ka nuqsan ka hisaab alag hai.

P-5.36 marks

Define a convex function. Prove that f(w) = |w| is minimised at w = 0 using the optimality conditions for non-differentiable convex functions, and explain why f′(w) = 0 cannot be used.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(a) Convex function ki tareef dein.
(b) Saabit karein ke f(w) = |w| ka sab se chhota point w = 0 par hai — magar un shartein se jo non-differentiable (nukeele mod wale) functions ke liye hain.
(c) Batayein ke purana tareeqa f′(w) = 0 yahan kyun kaam nahi karta.

(Ye topic lectures mein poora hai magar abhi tak koi sawal nahi aaya — is liye aane ka imkaan zyada hai.)

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Definition. f is convex if for all w, z and α ∈ [0,1]:

f( alpha w + (1-alpha) z ) <= alpha f(w) + (1-alpha) f(z)

i.e. every chord lies on or above the graph; equivalently f″ ≥ 0 where it exists.

Why f′ = 0 cannot be used: |w| has a kink at 0 — slope −1 on the left, +1 on the right — so the derivative does not exist there, and it vanishes nowhere else either. The smooth condition would wrongly conclude no minimum exists.

CORRECT CONDITIONS (one-sided derivatives always exist if f is convex): w minimises f <=> f'_-(w) <= 0 AND f'_+(w) >= 0 For f(w) = |w| at w = 0, f(0) = 0 : f'_+(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim t/t = +1 (t > 0) f'_-(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim -t/t = -1 (t < 0) f'_-(0) = -1 <= 0 ok f'_+(0) = +1 >= 0 ok Both hold => w = 0 minimises |w|. QED

Why it matters: this is the exact tool that proves the median minimises MAE and the τ-quantile minimises tilted loss, and it explains why the kink in |θj| holds lasso coefficients at exactly zero.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Tareef: ek function convex hai agar uska graph pyale (bowl) ki tarah oopar ko muda hua ho. Formal shakal:

f( alpha w + (1-alpha) z ) <= alpha f(w) + (1-alpha) f(z)

Iska aasaan matlab: graph par koi bhi do point lein aur un ke beech seedhi line (chord) khench dein. Agar wo line hamesha curve ke oopar (ya us par) rahe, to function convex hai. Ya derivative ki zubaan mein: f″ ≥ 0 — yaani curvature hamesha oopar ki taraf.

(c) Purana tareeqa kyun kaam nahi karta:

|w| ka graph "V" ki shakal ka hai. 0 par ek nukeela mod hai. Bayein taraf dhalaan −1 hai, dayein taraf +1. Ye dono barabar nahi, is liye 0 par derivative mojood hi nahi. Aur kisi doosri jagah bhi derivative 0 nahi hota (wo hamesha −1 ya +1 hi hai). To agar hum purana tareeqa lagayein (f′ = 0 dhoondo), to jawab aayega "koi minimum nahi hai" — jo saaf ghalat hai, kyunki nazar aa raha hai ke 0 par sab se neeche hai.

(b) Theek shartein — dono taraf se alag alag derivative lein:

w minimum hai AGAR f'_-(w) <= 0 AUR f'_+(w) >= 0 f(w) = |w| ke liye, w = 0 par (jahan f(0) = 0): DAYEIN se (t musbat, to |t| = t): f'_+(0) = [ |t| - 0 ] / t = t/t = +1 BAYEIN se (t manfi, to |t| = -t): f'_-(0) = [ |t| - 0 ] / t = -t/t = -1 Ab shartein check karein: f'_-(0) = -1 kya <= 0 hai? HAAN f'_+(0) = +1 kya >= 0 hai? HAAN Dono poori => w = 0 par minimum hai. SABIT

Shart ka aasaan matlab: "f′− ≤ 0" ka matlab hai "bayein ki taraf se aate hue function neeche aa raha tha" — yaani peeche hatne ka faida nahi. "f′+ ≥ 0" ka matlab "aage jaane par oopar chala jaayega" — yaani aage badhne ka bhi faida nahi. Jab dono taraf nuqsan hai, to aap sab se neeche khade hain. Bas.

Ye kyun ahem hai: bilkul yehi hathiyar ye saabit karta hai ke median absolute loss ko minimize karta hai aur τ-quantile tilted loss ko (dono mein nukeele mod hote hain). Aur yehi batata hai ke lasso mein |θj| ka mod coefficients ko exactly zero par kaise pakad leta hai.

P-5.46 marks

Derive the optimal constant predictor under square loss, show the resulting risk equals the data variance, and state the practical use of this result.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(a) Square loss ke saath behtareen constant predictor derive karein (yaani formula se nikaalein, bata kar nahi).
(b) Dikhayein ke us par error, data ka variance ban jaata hai.
(c) Is nateeje ka amali faida kya hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

L(theta) = (1/n) SUM (theta - y_i)^2 L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 => n theta = SUM y_i => theta = (1/n) SUM y_i = the MEAN At that optimum: L(mean) = (1/n) SUM (mean - y_i)^2 = var(y) (by definition)

Practical use: it is the universal baseline for regression. The best predictor that ignores the features entirely achieves MSE equal to the data variance. Therefore:

  • Any model whose test MSE is not comfortably below the target variance has learned nothing useful.
  • It defines R² = 1 − MSE/var: R² = 0 means "as good as the constant"; R² < 0 on test data means worse — an overfitting warning.
  • It makes an absolute error figure meaningful: "MSE = 240" is worthless if the variance is 250, excellent if it is 10,000.

Generalises: under absolute loss the baseline is the median; in classification it is the majority-class predictor.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Derivation — qadam ba qadam:

Constant predictor ka matlab: har soorat mein wohi ek number "theta" bolna. Error (empirical risk): L(theta) = (1/n) SUM (theta - y_i)^2 Minimum wahan hoga jahan dhalaan 0 ho, to derivative lein: L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) Isay 0 ke barabar rakhein: SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 (theta + theta + ... n dafa) - (y_1 + ... + y_n) = 0 n * theta = SUM y_i theta = (1/n) SUM y_i <-- yehi MEAN hai

(b) Us par error = variance

theta = mean rakh kar: L(mean) = (1/n) SUM (mean - y_i)^2 Aur statistics mein isi cheez ka naam VARIANCE hai. To: L(mean) = var(y)

Yaani: "square loss ke saath behtareen akele number ka error, data ka variance hota hai."

(c) Amali faida — ye regression ka "pass marks" hai

Soch kar dekhein: ye wo model hai jo features ko dekhta hi nahi. Wo bas average bol deta hai. Aur uska error = variance.

To ab aap ke paas ek paimana aa gaya:

  • Agar aap ka taqatwar model, poori mehnat ke baad, variance se kam MSE nahi de raha — to usne kuch bhi faidemand nahi seekha. Wo "average bol dene" se behtar nahi hai.
  • Isi se mash-hoor R² banta hai: R² = 1 − MSE/variance. Yaani R² = 0 ka matlab "constant jitna hi acha", aur test par R² negative aa jaaye to matlab constant se bhi bura — ye overfitting ki saaf tanbeeh hai.
  • Aur akela error number maani-khez ban jaata hai: "MSE = 240" sun kar kuch pata nahi chalta. Magar agar variance 250 ho to ye bay-kaar model hai; aur agar variance 10,000 ho to ye shaandaar hai.

Yaad rakhein: koi bhi model banane se pehle ye baseline nikaal lein. Absolute loss ke saath baseline median hota hai; aur classification mein wohi kaam "sab se bari class bol dena" karta hai (dekhein P-4.3 ka 99.8%).

P-5.56 marks

An energy retailer faces billing disputes over percentage errors: 20 kWh wrong matters far more for a small household. Which loss do the lectures provide, what is its optimal constant for y = (1, 4), and what familiar quantity is it?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek bijli company ke jhagde percentage ghalti par hote hain. 20 unit ki ghalti chhote ghar ke liye bohat bari baat hai (unka kul bill hi 25 unit ka tha!), magar bade ghar ke liye mamooli (unka bill 2,500 unit ka hai).

(a) Lectures kaunsa loss dete hain?
(b) y = (1, 4) ke liye uska behtareen constant kya hai?
(c) Wo kis jaani-pehchani cheez ke barabar nikalta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Loss: fractional loss (for ŷ, y > 0):

ell(y-hat, y) = max( y-hat/y - 1 , y/y-hat - 1 ) = exp|log y-hat - log y| - 1

Why it fits: square and absolute loss penalise the difference, so 20 kWh wrong costs the same on a 25 kWh household (80% wrong) as on a 2,500 kWh one (0.8% wrong). Fractional loss penalises the ratio.

theta = ( SUM_(i<=k) y_i / SUM_(i>k) 1/y_i )^(1/2) n = 2, k = 1 : theta = ( 1 / 0.25 )^(1/2) = 4^(1/2) = 2 check 1 <= 2 <= 4 ok

The familiar quantity: the geometric mean — √(1×4) = 2. (Arithmetic mean would be 2.5.) Not a coincidence: fractional loss measures distance on a logarithmic scale, and averaging in log-space then transforming back is the geometric mean.

Practical equivalent: fit ordinary squared loss to log(y) — it penalises proportional errors similarly and keeps the least-squares machinery.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Loss: fractional loss (sirf jab dono numbers musbat hon):

ell(y-hat, y) = max( y-hat/y - 1 , y/y-hat - 1 )

Kyun ye munasib hai — numbers se dekhein:

Chhota ghar: asal 25, model ne kaha 45 -> 20 ki ghalti, magar 80% ghalat Bara ghar: asal 2500, model ne kaha 2520 -> 20 ki ghalti, magar 0.8% ghalat Square loss dono ko: (20)^2 = 400 --> BARABAR bura samajhta hai Absolute loss dono ko: 20 --> BARABAR bura samajhta hai Magar haqeeqat mein pehla bohat zyada bura hai! Fractional loss nisbat dekhta hai, is liye wo pehle ko bohat bura kehta hai.

(b) Hisaab — y = (1, 4)

Formula: theta = ( chhote points ka jama / bade points ke 1/y ka jama )^(1/2) n = 2, k = 1 lein: oopar wala hissa = y_1 = 1 neeche wala hissa = 1/y_2 = 1/4 = 0.25 theta = ( 1 / 0.25 )^(1/2) = ( 4 )^(1/2) = 2 Check karein: jawab 1 aur 4 ke darmiyan hona chahiye. 1 <= 2 <= 4 theek

(c) Ye kis cheez ke barabar hai: GEOMETRIC MEAN

geometric mean = sqrt( 1 x 4 ) = sqrt(4) = 2 <-- wohi jawab! Moazne ke liye: aam average (arithmetic mean) = (1 + 4)/2 = 2.5 fractional middle = 2

Ye ittefaq nahi hai. Fractional loss faasla log ke paimane par naapta hai. Aur log ki duniya mein average lena, phir wapas aana — bas yehi geometric mean hota hai.

Amali shortcut (exam mein likhein): fractional loss ka code likhne ke bajaye log target par log laga kar aam squared loss fit kar lete hain — yaani log(y) predict karein. Asar taqreeban wohi hai, aur least squares ki saari sahulat barqaraar rehti hai. (Yehi tarkeeb gharon ki keemat wale sawal mein bhi thi.)

P-5.66 marks

Why is ℒ(θ) convex whenever the loss is convex, why does it matter, and what would change otherwise? Name a non-convex loss from the lectures.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(a) Agar loss convex ho, to poora ℒ(θ) (average loss) bhi convex kyun ho jaata hai?
(b) Ye baat ahem kyun hai?
(c) Agar convex na hota to kya badal jaata?
(d) Lectures se ek non-convex loss ka naam dein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Three-step argument: (1) the standard losses are convex in ŷ; (2) ŷi = θTxi is affine in θ, and a convex function of an affine map stays convex; (3) an average of convex functions is convex, and ℒ is such an average.

Why it matters: every local minimum is global; the optimality conditions are sufficient as well as necessary (which licenses the clean mean/median/quantile derivations); results are reproducible regardless of initialisation; and the problem is solvable efficiently — hence the lectures' remark that the lasso has no formula "but we can efficiently compute it anyway (since it's convex)".

Without convexity: multiple local minima and saddle points → the answer depends on initialisation, there is no certificate of optimality, and results are not reproducible.

Non-convex example: log-Huber. Beyond α its derivative 2α²/r decreases, so p″ < 0. Its extra robustness is bought by sacrificing convexity. (Similarly ℓ0.5 is non-convex, so computing it is only heuristic.)

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Teen qadam ki daleel:

  1. Jo aam losses hain (square, absolute, tilted, Huber) wo sab convex hain.
  2. ŷ = θTx — ye θ ke lehaz se seedha (affine) hai. Aur convex function ke andar seedhi cheez daalne se wo convex hi rehta hai.
  3. Aur convex functions ka average bhi convex hota hai. Aur ℒ to average hi hai (n losses ka).

Teen qadam, kaam khatam: ℒ(θ) convex hai.

(b) Ye kyun ahem hai — chaar faide:

  • Jo gaddha mila, wohi sab se gehra hai. Convex function mein sirf ek gaddha hota hai. To jo minimum mila, usi par bharosa kar sakte hain.
  • Shartein "kaafi" hain, sirf "zaroori" nahi. Yaani ∇ℒ = 0 dekh kar hum saabit kar sakte hain ke yehi jawab hai. Isi wajah se mean/median/quantile ke saaf saboot mumkin hue.
  • Jawab dohraya ja sakta hai. Do bande alag alag jagah se shuru karein, jawab wohi aayega.
  • Computer jaldi hal kar leta hai. Isi liye lecture lasso ke baare mein kehta hai: "formula nahi hai, magar phir bhi hum usay efficiently nikaal lete hain, kyunki wo convex hai."

(c) Agar convex na hota: kai gaddhe hote. Model jahan se shuru hota, wahin ke qareeb wale gaddhe mein gir jaata — aur usay pata bhi nahi chalta ke kahin aur zyada gehra gaddha mojood hai. Jawab shuruati guess par depend karta, koi saboot na hota, aur do log do mukhtalif jawab nikaal laate.

(d) Non-convex misaal: log-Huber. α ke baad uska derivative 2α²/r hai, jo r barhne par ghatta hai — yaani curvature ulti taraf mud gayi (p″ < 0). Uski zyada robustness convexity ki qurbani de kar kharidi gayi hai.

Yaad rakhein: ℓ0.5 regularizer bhi non-convex hai — isi liye lecture kehta hai ke usay nikalna sirf heuristic hai (yaani zamanat nahi). Aur ℓ1 (lasso) wo sab se chhoti power hai jo phir bhi convex hai — isi liye wo itna mash-hoor hua.

P-5.76 marks

A pharmacy model predicts drug dose. Overdosing is life-threatening; under-dosing merely reduces efficacy. Which loss, and what range for its parameter? Justify the direction with an explicit sign calculation.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek model dawa ki khuraak batata hai. Zyada khuraak jaan-leva hai; kam khuraak se sirf asar kam hota hai (agli baar theek kiya ja sakta hai).

(a) Kaunsa loss use karein?
(b) Uske parameter (τ) ki kaunsi range?
(c) Nishaan (sign) ka hisaab kar ke wajah dein — andaza na lagayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Loss: tilted absolute loss with τ < ½.

SIGN CHECK. r = y-hat - y correct dose y = 40 mg, model predicts y-hat = 55 mg r = 55 - 40 = +15 (POSITIVE) -> too much -> OVERDOSE => OVERDOSE = over-estimation = r > 0 <- dangerous side WHICH tau PENALISES r > 0 MORE? r >= 0 costs (1-tau)|r| ; r < 0 costs tau|r| need (1 - tau) > tau => 1 > 2 tau => tau < 1/2 tau = 0.1 : 10 mg overdose -> 0.9(10) = 9 10 mg under-dose -> 0.1(10) = 1 -> 9 : 1

The optimal constant is the τ-quantile — at τ = 0.1, the 10th percentile, a deliberately conservative dose so only ~10% of patients are over-dosed.

Sanity check: penalising overdose should make the model prescribe less; a small τ gives a low quantile. ✓

Caveats: τ encodes a clinical judgement, so set it with clinicians; and a quantile loss gives asymmetry but not a hard limit — for a true ceiling add an explicit constraint.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: tilted absolute loss, aur τ aadhe se KAM (τ < ½).

Qadam 1 — nishaan pakka karein (andaza na lagayein, numbers daalein):

r = y-hat - y (andaza minus sach) Farz karein sahi khuraak y = 40 mg hai aur model kehta hai y-hat = 55 mg r = 55 - 40 = +15 (POSITIVE) Patient ko 55 mg mil gayi jabke 40 chahiye thi -> ZYADA khuraak -> OVERDOSE To: OVERDOSE = zyada batana = r > 0 <-- ye khatarnak taraf hai

Qadam 2 — kaunsa τ is taraf saza deta hai:

Tilted loss ki qeematein: r >= 0 (zyada bataya) ki qeemat = (1 - tau) |r| r < 0 (kam bataya) ki qeemat = tau |r| Hamein chahiye ke r > 0 ki qeemat ZYADA ho: (1 - tau) > tau 1 > 2 tau tau < 1/2 <-- yehi range chahiye

Qadam 3 — ek number daal kar check karein (τ = 0.1):

10 mg ZYADA de di : qeemat = (1 - 0.1)(10) = 0.9 x 10 = 9 10 mg KAM de di : qeemat = (0.1)(10) = 0.1 x 10 = 1 Nisbat 9 : 1 -> overdose nau guna mehnga. Bilkul theek.

Jawab: behtareen constant = τ-quantile. τ = 0.1 par ye 10th percentile hai — yaani jaan boojh kar kam khuraak, taake sirf taqreeban 10% patients ko zyada mile.

Aqal se tasdeeq (ye zaroor karein): agar hum overdose par saza laga rahe hain, to model ko kam khuraak likhni chahiye. Aur chhota τ neecha quantile deta hai, yaani kam khuraak. ✓ Mel kha gaya.

Yaad rakhein: ye OHT Q-8 (food delivery) ka ulta case hai — wahan kam batana bura tha (late delivery) to τ > ½ chahiye tha; yahan zyada batana bura hai to τ < ½. Isi liye nishaan ka hisaab hamesha likh kar karein, zehen se nahi. Aur ye bhi likh dein ke τ doctors ke saath tay karna chahiye — ye tibbi faisla hai, riyaazi nahi.

P-5.86 marks

Three teams fit a constant predictor to the same data and report 42, 38 and 51, with no arithmetic errors. How is this possible, and what single piece of information decides which is correct?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Teen teams ne bilkul ek hi data par constant predictor fit kiya aur teen mukhtalif jawab diye: 42, 38, 51. Aur kisi ne hisaab mein ghalti nahi ki.

(a) Ye kaise mumkin hai?
(b) Kaunsi ek cheez bata degi ke kaun theek hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

They used different loss functions. "What is the best single number?" has no answer until the loss is specified, because the loss defines "best" — the lectures' point that "different losses lead to different predictors".

  • 42 = the mean → square loss.
  • 38 = the median → absolute loss.
  • 51 = an upper τ-quantile (τ > ½) → tilted loss.

The ordering is informative: median (38) < mean (42) means the data is right-skewed — a few large values pulling the mean up — typical of durations, prices and incomes.

The information needed: the loss function — i.e. how the organisation values the direction and magnitude of error. Clean data, large errors disproportionately damaging → 42. Outliers present → 38. Under-estimation much worse → 51.

Lesson: the loss is a modelling decision encoding business values, not a default to inherit.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab (a): un teeno ne mukhtalif LOSS use kiya.

Kyun: sawal "behtareen akela number kya hai?" ka koi jawab nahi jab tak aap ye na batayein ke "behtareen" ka matlab kya hai. Aur "behtareen" ki tareef loss karta hai. Lecture ka jumla yaad karein: "mukhtalif losses se mukhtalif predictors nikalte hain."

  • 42 = mean (average) → square loss use hua.
  • 38 = median (beech wala) → absolute loss use hua.
  • 51 = ek ooncha quantile → tilted loss, τ > ½ ke saath.

Ek bonus baat (likhne par extra mark): in ki tarteeb khud ek saboot hai. Median (38) mean (42) se chhota hai. Iska matlab data mein kuch badi values hain jo mean ko oopar kheench rahi hain (right-skewed). Ye waqt, keematon aur income mein bilkul aam hai.

Jawab (b) — jo cheez chahiye: loss function — yaani company ye batay ke wo kis qism ki ghalti ko kitna bura samajhti hai:

  • Data saaf hai aur badi ghalti bohat nuqsan-deh → 42 theek.
  • Data mein outliers hain → 38 theek.
  • Kam batana bohat zyada bura hai (jaise delivery ka wada) → 51 theek.

Yaad rakhein: loss ek faisla hai, default nahi. Jab koi kehta hai "hum ne squared error use kiya" — wo asal mein ye keh raha hai "mere liye dono taraf ki ghalti barabar buri hai, aur badi ghalti chaar-guna buri hai". Aur bohat se kaamon mein ye baat sach nahi hoti.

Set 6 — 5 sawal

Robust fitting (outliers ka ilaaj)

Poore hisse ka ek hi khayal: square loss ka zor 2r hai, jo bay-had barhta hai — is liye ek kharab point poore model ko kheench leta hai.

P-6.16 marks

With α = 2, tabulate the square and Huber penalties and their derivatives at r = 1, 2, 5, 20. Explain in one sentence why Huber is robust, and verify the two branches agree at r = α.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

α = 2 ke saath square aur Huber penalty ka table banayein aur un ke derivatives bhi, r = 1, 2, 5, 20 par. Phir ek jumle mein batayein Huber robust kyun hai. Aur tasdeeq karein ke r = α par Huber ke dono tukde mil jaate hain.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

r p_sqr p_hub p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub ---- ------- --------- ------------ -------- 1 1 1 2 2 2 4 4 4 4 5 25 16 10 4 (2.5x weaker) 20 400 76 40 4 (10x weaker) BRANCHES AGREE AT r = alpha = 2: value: r^2 = 4 vs alpha(2r - alpha) = 2(4-2) = 4 ok (continuous) slope: 2r = 4 vs 2 alpha = 4 ok (smooth, C1)

One sentence: beyond |r| = α the Huber derivative saturates at the constant 2α = 4, so every outlier exerts the same bounded pull however extreme it is — whereas 2r grows without bound, letting one far-off point dominate the gradient.

Why the smoothness matters: Huber is both convex and C1, so optimization is well behaved with a unique global minimum — unlike absolute loss (kinked at 0) and log-Huber (smooth but non-convex).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Huber ka formula: |r| ≤ 2 par r²; |r| > 2 par 2(2|r| − 2).

r p_sqr p_hub p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub ---- ------- -------- ------------ -------- 1 1 1 2 2 2 4 4 4 4 5 25 16 10 4 (2.5 guna kam) 20 400 76 40 4 (10 guna kam)

Table padhne ka tareeqa — dayein do columns asal jawab hain. Loss ki value se zyada ahem uska derivative hai, kyunki gradient mein har point ka zor usi derivative ke mutabiq hota hai.

Dekhein: r = 20 par square ka zor 40 hai, magar Huber ka sirf 4 — aur wo 4 par ruk gaya hai, aage nahi barhega.

Ek jumla (jawab mein yehi likhein): α ke baad Huber ka derivative 2α = 4 par ruk jaata hai, is liye har outlier barabar aur mehdood zor lagata hai — chahe wo kitna bhi door ho — jabke square loss ka zor 2r bay-had barhta rehta hai aur ek door ka point poore model par qabza kar leta hai.

Dono tukde r = 2 par milte hain — tasdeeq:

VALUE check (koi chhalaang nahi honi chahiye): quadratic tukda : r^2 = 2^2 = 4 affine tukda : 2(2r - 2) = 2(4-2) = 2(2) = 4 Dono 4 -> curve mein koi toot-foot nahi (continuous) DHALAAN check (koi nukeela mod nahi hona chahiye): quadratic tukda ka derivative : 2r = 2(2) = 4 affine tukda ka derivative : 2 alpha = 2(2) = 4 Dono 4 -> mod bhi nahi (smooth)

Ye smoothness kyun ahem hai: Huber convex bhi hai aur smooth bhi — yaani ek hi gaddha, aur koi nukeela mod nahi. Is liye optimization bilkul bharosay ke qabil hai. Absolute loss convex hai magar 0 par mod rakhta hai; log-Huber smooth hai magar convex nahi. Huber akela hai jis mein teeno khoobiyan hain — isi liye wo robust fitting ka default chunaav hai.

P-6.26 marks

One colleague sets α = 10,000 where typical residuals are ~2 and reports that robustness "made no difference". Another sets α = 0.001. What has each actually fitted, and how should α be chosen?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek banda Huber loss mein α = 10,000 rakh deta hai (jabke aam ghaltiyan taqreeban 2 ke aas paas hain) aur kehta hai "robustness se koi farq nahi pada". Doosra banda α = 0.001 rakh deta hai.

(a) Dono ne asal mein kaunsa loss fit kiya?
(b) α kaise chunna chahiye?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

α = 10,000 → they fitted ordinary least squares. Every residual satisfies |r| < α, so every point sits on the quadratic branch and phub = r² throughout. The affine branch is never reached, so robustness is never activated — their observation is exactly correct. As α → ∞, Huber → square loss.

α = 0.001 → they fitted (scaled) absolute loss. Almost every residual exceeds α, so phub ≈ 2α|r| — a constant times |r|, and scaling does not move the minimiser. As α → 0, Huber → absolute loss.

How to choose: α is the boundary between ordinary noise and outlier, so set it near the scale of genuine noise. Primary method: validation on a log grid. Good starting point: 1–2× the median absolute deviation of the residuals — use MAD not the standard deviation, since the standard deviation is itself inflated by the outliers you are guarding against.

Here, α ≈ 2–4 would be sensible — not 10,000 and not 0.001.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle Huber ka formula yaad karein: |r| ≤ α par r² (square jaisa), aur |r| > α par seedhi line. Yaani α wo sarhad hai jahan square khatam aur seedhi line shuru hoti hai.

(a) Pehla banda — α = 10,000: us ne asal mein aam square loss fit kiya.

Kyun: ghaltiyan 2 ke aas paas hain, aur shadeed outliers bhi 100-200 tak honge. Koi bhi ghalti 10,000 se bari nahi. To har point |r| ≤ α wale hisse mein aa gaya, yaani har point par r² lagta raha. Seedhi line wala hissa kabhi aaya hi nahi! To robustness chalu hi nahi hui. Us banday ki baat bilkul theek hai — farq waqai nahi pada, kyunki us ne Huber ko naam ka Huber rakha aur kaam square loss ka karwaya.

Qanoon: α bohat bara → Huber square loss ban jaata hai.

(a) Doosra banda — α = 0.001: us ne asal mein absolute loss fit kiya.

Kyun: ab ulta masla hai — taqreeban har ghalti 0.001 se bari hai. To har point seedhi line wale hisse mein chala gaya, jahan penalty taqreeban 2α|r| hai — yaani ek chhota constant guna |r|. Aur poore objective ko constant se guna karne se minimum ki jagah nahi badalti. To natija absolute loss ka hai.

Qanoon: α bohat chhota → Huber absolute loss ban jaata hai.

Yaani α ek safar karata hai: bohat chhota = absolute loss, darmiyana = asli Huber, bohat bara = square loss. Aur dono siron par wo bay-kaar hai.

(b) α kaise chunein:

Soch ye hai ke α wo had hai jahan "aam shor" khatam aur "outlier" shuru hota hai. To usay asli shor ke paimane par rakhein.

  1. Asal tareeqa: validation. α ko hyper-parameter maanein, kai values aazmayein, aur jo held-out error kam kare wo chunein.
  2. Shuruati andaza: residuals ke median absolute deviation (MAD) ka 1–2 guna. Khaas baat: MAD lein, standard deviation nahi — kyunki standard deviation khud unhi outliers se phool jaati hai jin se aap bachna chahte hain!

Is sawal mein: ghaltiyan ~2 hain, to α ≈ 2 se 4 munasib hoga — na 10,000, na 0.001.

P-6.36 marks

State the lectures' iterative outlier-removal procedure, give two reasons a robust penalty is generally preferable, and one situation where deletion is right.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Lectures ek tareeqa batate hain jis mein outliers ko baar baar hata kar dobara fit kiya jaata hai.

(a) Wo tareeqa likhein.
(b) Do wajuhat dein ke robust penalty (Huber) aam tor par behtar kyun hai.
(c) Ek soorat batayein jahan points hatana hi theek hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The procedure: (1) fit a predictor; (2) flag points with large prediction errors as outliers; (3) remove them and repeat.

Reason 1 — the deletion criterion comes from the fit the outliers already corrupted. On the first pass the model is fitted including the outliers, so it has already been dragged toward them — and that displaced fit then decides which points look "large". A strong outlier can pull the line far enough that nearby valid points appear anomalous while the outlier looks acceptable (masking and swamping). Iterating can progressively delete good data.

Reason 2 — deletion is a hard, irreversible decision needing an arbitrary threshold. A point just over it is discarded entirely; just under it keeps full quadratic influence. There is no principled cut, and iterating may strip a genuine tail — which biases the model, since real extreme values are legitimate data. Huber instead caps influence smoothly and keeps all the data.

When deletion is right: when points are provably invalid, not merely extreme — physically impossible values (negative age, 200°C body temperature), a sensor logged as faulty, or clear decimal-point corruption.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Tareeqa (lectures se):

  1. Poore data se model fit karein.
  2. Jin points ki prediction error bohat badi ho, unko "outlier" ka nishaan laga dein.
  3. Unko data se nikaal dein, aur phir qadam 1 se dobara shuru karein.

(b) Wajah 1 — jo adalat faisla kar rahi hai, usi ko mulzim ne khareed liya hai.

Ghaur karein: pehle chakkar mein model outliers ke saath fit hua. Yaani line pehle hi un ki taraf kheench li ja chuki hai. Aur ab wohi khisaki hui line faisla kar rahi hai ke kaun "bohat door" hai!

Natija bohat bura ho sakta hai: ek taqatwar outlier line ko itna kheench le ke us ke paas ke sahi points door lagne lagein, aur outlier khud theek lage. Phir aap sahi data phenk dein aur kharab rakh lein. (Is ko masking aur swamping kehte hain.) Aur ye chakkar dohrate rehne se acha data thoda thoda katta jaata hai.

(b) Wajah 2 — hatana ek sakht, wapas na hone wala faisla hai, aur us ke liye ek bay-maani had chahiye.

Farz karein had 3 rakh di. Ab jiska residual 3.01 hai wo poora phenk diya jaata hai, aur jiska 2.99 hai wo poora gina jaata hai. Ye bay-insaafi hai, aur us had ki koi usooli jagah nahi. Aur ek bara khatra: data ki asli dum (tail) kat sakti hai — yaani wo badi values jo waqai hoti hain (jaise waqai koi mehnga ghar), aur unko phenkne se model jhuk jaata hai.

Huber is ke bajaye kisi ko phenkta nahi — wo sirf us ke asar par had laga deta hai, narmi se. Saara data bacha rehta hai, aur sirf ek knob (α) validation se tune ho jaata hai.

(c) Hatana kab theek hai: jab point sabit shuda ghalat ho, sirf bara na ho.

  • Jismani tor par namumkin value — negative umar, jism ka darja 200°C.
  • Wo sensor jis ki kharabi record mein likhi hui hai (jaise "us din cable dheeli thi").
  • Saaf decimal point ki ghalti — aur aksar aise case mein usay theek kiya ja sakta hai, phenkne ki zaroorat bhi nahi.

Yaad rakhein — asal farq: bahar ki maaloomat se pata chale ke point ghalat hai → hata dein. Point sirf bara lag raha ho → robust loss lagayein. Dono ek doosre ke dushman nahi — pehle sabit shuda kharab records nikaalein, phir bachay hue data par Huber lagayein.

P-6.46 marks

Choose between absolute, Huber and log-Huber loss for data with a few extremely corrupted readings. Compare on robustness, convexity and smoothness, and give a recommended procedure.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Aise data ke liye jis mein chand bohat hi kharab readings hain, teen mein se chunein: absolute, Huber, log-Huber. Teeno ka moazna karein (robustness, convexity, smoothness) aur ek amali tareeqa tajweez karein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

AbsoluteHuberLog-Huber
Large-r derivative±1±2α (capped)2α²/r — decays to 0
RobustnessGoodGoodBest
Convex?YesYesNo
Smooth?No — kink at 0Yes (C1)Yes
Small errorsPenalty relatively largeQuadraticQuadratic

Recommended procedure:

  1. Start with Huber — the only one robust and convex and smooth. α near 1–2× the MAD, by validation.
  2. Check the residual histogram and held-out error. Since corruption is extreme, Huber's capped-but-nonzero pull of 2α per outlier may still bias the fit.
  3. If so, escalate to log-Huber — but initialise from the Huber solution. Solving the convex problem first makes the non-convex one far likelier to land in the right basin.
  4. Compare candidates on held-out error, as the lectures prescribe.
  5. Separately remove provably invalid records.

Why not plain absolute loss? Robust, convex and parameter-free, but its penalty is relatively large for small residuals, so it fits the clean majority less tightly, and its kink at 0 complicates gradient methods.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

AbsoluteHuberLog-Huber
Bade r par zor±1 — pakka±2α — ruk jaata hai2α²/r — 0 tak ghatta hai
RobustnessAchiAchiSab se behtar
Convex?HaanHaanNahi
Smooth?Nahi — 0 par modHaanHaan
Chhoti ghalti parJurmana nisbatan zyadaSquare jaisaSquare jaisa
