Table ka khulasa: jitni zyada robustness chahiye, utni optimization ki aasani qurban karni padti hai.

Tajweez — qadam ba qadam:

  1. Huber se shuru karein. Wajah: teeno mein sirf yehi robust aur convex aur smooth — teeno hai. α ko MAD ka 1–2 guna rakhein aur validation se tune karein.
  2. Phir natija dekhein — residuals ka histogram aur held-out error. Kyunki kharabi bohat shadeed hai, Huber ka zor (2α) mehdood to hai magar sifar nahi — aur agar aise kai points hon to wo mil kar fit ko phir bhi jhuka sakte hain.
  3. Zaroorat pade to log-Huber par jaayein — magar Huber ke jawab se shuru karein. Ye sab se ahem amali nukta hai: pehle convex masla hal karein (jis ka jawab pakka sahi hai), aur usi jawab ko log-Huber ka starting point banayein. Is se non-convex masle mein sahi gaddhe mein girne ka imkaan bohat barh jaata hai.
  4. Aakhir mein teeno ka moazna held-out error par karein, jaisa lectures kehte hain.
  5. Aur alag se wo records nikaal dein jo sabit shuda ghalat hain (P-6.3 dekhein).

Saada absolute loss kyun nahi? Wo robust hai, convex hai, aur us mein koi knob bhi nahi (aasani!). Magar do kharabiyan: chhoti ghaltiyon par uska jurmana nisbatan zyada hai, to wo saaf data ko utna kas kar fit nahi karta jitna Huber karta hai; aur 0 par uska nukeela mod gradient methods ko mushkil bana deta hai.

P-6.56 marks

How does the penalty choice shape the residual histogram, and what would you conclude from: (a) symmetric with two long tails; (b) most residuals slightly negative; (c) residuals growing with one feature; (d) a tall spike at zero with a few very large values?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Penalty ka chunaav residuals ke histogram ki shakal kaise badalta hai? (Histogram = saari ghaltiyon ko khaano mein baant kar bar graph bana dena.)

Aur in chaar shaklon se kya natija nikaalenge:
(a) 0 ke around symmetric, magar dono taraf lambi dumein
(b) Zyadatar residuals thoday negative
(c) Residuals ek feature ke saath barhte hue
(d) 0 par ooncha spike, aur kuch bohat badi values

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

How: ERM minimises the average penalty, so the penalty is a statement about which distribution of errors is acceptable. Square loss punishes large residuals hard and small ones barely, spreading error symmetrically; robust penalties concentrate mass near zero and tolerate a few large values; tilted penalties deliberately shift the histogram to one side.

(a) Symmetric, two long tails → healthy centring but outliers. The signature of square loss on contaminated data. Action: switch to Huber.

(b) Mostly slightly negative → r < 0 means systematic under-prediction. If a tilted loss with τ < ½ was used, this is intended. If a symmetric loss was used it is a bias — most likely a missing or wrongly regularized offset.

(c) Growing with one feature → underfitting / misspecification. Residuals should be random against every input. Action: enrich the feature map — a robust loss would not help.

(d) Tall spike at zero + a few large values → the fingerprint of a successful robust fit, and a good result: the bulk fitted tightly, a few outliers deliberately abandoned.

Rule: shifted → bias/asymmetric loss. Patterned against a feature → fix the features. Heavy-tailed → fix the loss.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle "kaise" samjhein: ERM average penalty ko sab se chhota karta hai. Yaani penalty chun kar aap asal mein ye elaan kar rahe hain ke "ghaltiyon ki kaunsi shakal mujhe qubool hai". Aur phir model wohi shakal bana kar de deta hai:

  • Square loss: badi ghalti par sakhti, chhoti par narmi → wo error ko phaila deta hai, koi ek ghalti bohat badi nahi hone deta (chahe baqi sab thodi kharab ho jaayein).
  • Robust penalties (Huber): 0 ke paas jhund bana deti hain aur chand badi ghaltiyan bardasht kar leti hain.
  • Tilted penalty: jaan boojh kar poora histogram ek taraf khisaka deta hai.

(a) Symmetric magar dono taraf lambi dumein

Natija: markaz theek hai (koi bias nahi) — magar outliers mojood hain. Ye kharab data par square loss ki pehchan hai. Amal: Huber par aa jaayein, aur un dum wale points ko dekhein ke wo waqai kharab hain ya asli badi values.

(b) Zyadatar thoday negative

Natija: r = ŷ − y hai, to r < 0 ka matlab model ba-qaida kam bata raha hai. Ab do soorat:

  • Agar aap ne tilted loss (τ < ½) use kiya tha → ye bilkul irada tha, koi masla nahi.
  • Agar symmetric loss tha → ye ek bias hai. Sab se zyada imkaan: offset missing hai, ya usay ghalti se regularize kar diya gaya (dekhein P-7.3).

(c) Residuals ek feature ke saath barhte hue

Natija: underfitting — model mein kuch structure daalna bhool gaye. Residuals har input ke khilaaf be-tarteeb hone chahiye; agar tarteeb hai to matlab us feature ka rishta seedha nahi hai. Amal: features theek karein (square, log, hinge, interaction). Robust loss yahan bilkul kaam nahi aayegi — ye outlier ka masla nahi hai.

(d) 0 par ooncha spike + kuch badi values

Natija: ye kaamyaab robust fit ki pehchan hai — aur ye acha natija hai, masla nahi! Matlab: model ne zyadatar data ko kas kar fit kar liya (spike), aur chand outliers ko jaan boojh kar chhod diya (badi values). Lecture ka jumla: "a robust predictor might fit 98% of the data very well."

Yaad rakhein — chaar shaklein, chaar nataij: symmetric aur tight → sab theek. Ek taraf khisak gaya → bias ya offset dekhein. Kisi feature ke khilaaf pattern → features theek karein. Lambi dumein → loss theek karein.

Set 7 — 7 sawal

Regularization aur sparsity

Poore hisse ka khulasa: ridge ka dhakka zero ke paas khatam ho jaata hai, lasso ka qaayam rehta hai. Isi ek baat se taqreeban saare jawab nikalte hain.

P-7.16 marks

For θ = (1, −2, 2) and ρ = 0.5, compute the worst-case sensitivity under (a) an ℓ2 and (b) an ℓ∞ perturbation set. Exhibit the worst-case δ in each, and state which regularizer each justifies.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Farz karein model ke weights hain θ = (1, −2, 2), aur features mein ρ = 0.5 tak ki gadbad (shor) aa sakti hai. Dekhein sab se bure soorat mein prediction kitni bigad sakti hai:

(a) Jab gadbad ki kul lambai 0.5 tak ho (ℓ2)
(b) Jab har feature alag alag 0.5 tak bigad sake (ℓ∞)

Dono mein wo sab se bura δ bhi dikhayein, aur batayein kaunsa regularizer kis se nikalta hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

NORMS: ||theta||_2 = sqrt(1+4+4) = 3 ||theta||_1 = 1+2+2 = 5 (a) L2 SET { ||delta||_2 <= 0.5 } worst case = rho * ||theta||_2 = 0.5 * 3 = 1.5 worst delta = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 = (0.1667, -0.3333, 0.3333) check ||delta||_2 = sqrt(1/36+4/36+4/36) = sqrt(0.25) = 0.5 ok check theta^T delta = 0.1667 + 0.6667 + 0.6667 = 1.50 ok (b) L-INFINITY SET { |delta_j| <= 0.5 for all j } worst case = rho * ||theta||_1 = 0.5 * 5 = 2.5 worst delta = rho * sign(theta) = (0.5, -0.5, 0.5) check ||delta||_inf = 0.5 ok check theta^T delta = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.0 = 2.50 ok L2 -> justifies RIDGE (sum square) Linf -> justifies LASSO (sum absolute)

Interpretation. Same θ, same ρ, but the worst case is 1.5 vs 2.5 — 67% larger. Geometrically the ℓ2 ball is inscribed in the ℓ∞ cube, so the cube contains extra corner directions lying outside the ball. In general ‖θ‖1 ≥ ‖θ‖2.

What it means: the choice of regularizer is a statement about how you believe features can be corrupted. Total error bounded → ridge. Each feature with its own sensor, all able to be at their worst at once → lasso.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle do norms nikaal lein:

||theta||_2 = sqrt( 1^2 + 2^2 + 2^2 ) = sqrt(9) = 3 ||theta||_1 = |1| + |-2| + |2| = 5

(a) L2 case — gol gaind (kul gadbad 0.5 tak)

worst case = rho x ||theta||_2 = 0.5 x 3 = 1.5 Sab se bura delta (theta ki hi taraf, usi direction mein): delta = 0.5 x (1, -2, 2) / 3 = (0.1667, -0.3333, 0.3333) Check 1 - ye qanooni hai? ||delta||_2 = sqrt(1/36 + 4/36 + 4/36) = sqrt(0.25) = 0.5 HAAN Check 2 - is se kitna bigda? theta^T delta = 0.1667 + 0.6667 + 0.6667 = 1.50 formula se mel khaya

(b) L-infinity case — chaukor dabba (har feature alag alag 0.5 tak)

worst case = rho x ||theta||_1 = 0.5 x 5 = 2.5 Sab se bura delta (har feature ko uske theta ke nishaan ki taraf poora dhakel dein): delta = 0.5 x sign(1, -2, 2) = (0.5, -0.5, 0.5) Check 1 - qanooni hai? har component 0.5 hai, had 0.5 thi HAAN Check 2 - kitna bigda? theta^T delta = 1(0.5) + (-2)(-0.5) + 2(0.5) = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.0 = 2.50 mel khaya

Natija: ℓ2 → ridge; ℓ∞ → lasso.

Ab matlab samjhein: wohi θ, wohi ρ — magar jawab 1.5 aur 2.5. Doosra 67% zyada khatarnak!

Kyun? Geometry se. Gol gaind chaukor dabbe ke andar fit hoti hai. Yaani dabbe mein wo kone bhi mojood hain jahan saare features ek saath apni poori had par hain — aur wo kone gaind se bahar hain. Zyada options, zyada bura natija.

Yaad rakhein — asal sabaq: regularizer ka chunaav asal mein ye faisla hai ke aap features kaise kharab hone ka yaqeen rakhte hain. Agar kul gadbad mehdood ho (ek bigdi to doosri kam bigdegi) → ridge. Agar har feature ka apna sensor ho aur sab ek saath bigad sakein — aur haqeeqat mein aksar yehi hota hai → lasso. Yaani ridge aur lasso ittefaq se nahi bane; dono ek hi sawal ke do jawab hain.

P-7.26 marks

Prove that the worst-case sensitivity of a linear predictor under an ℓ2 perturbation set equals ρ‖θ‖2. State where Cauchy-Schwarz is used and why the bound is attained.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Saabit karein ke jab features mein ℓ2 qism ki gadbad (had ρ) ho, to prediction sab se zyada ρ‖θ‖2 tak bigad sakti hai — is se zyada nahi, aur itna waqai mumkin hai.

(a) Saboot likhein.
(b) Batayein Cauchy-Schwarz kahan lagaya.
(c) Batayein ke ye had waqai haasil kyun ho jaati hai (yaani sirf upper bound nahi).

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

SETUP. The change in prediction when x -> x + delta is theta^T(x + delta) - theta^T x = theta^T delta (x cancels) S = max over { ||delta||_2 <= rho } of | theta^T delta | STEP 1 - UPPER BOUND (Cauchy-Schwarz is used HERE): | theta^T delta | <= ||theta||_2 ||delta||_2 <= ||theta||_2 * rho so S <= rho ||theta||_2 STEP 2 - THE BOUND IS ATTAINED. Take delta* = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 : admissible? ||delta*||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 / ||theta||_2 = rho ok value? theta^T delta* = rho (theta^T theta)/||theta||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 ok => S = rho * ||theta||_2 QED

Why it is attained: Cauchy-Schwarz holds with equality exactly when the vectors are parallel, and δ* is built parallel to θ. Intuitively the adversary's best move is to spend the whole budget pushing along the direction the predictor is most responsive to; any component orthogonal to θ contributes nothing while still consuming budget.

Purpose: ρ is a property of the world, not under our control, so the only way to reduce sensitivity is to make ‖θ‖2 small — this derives the sum-square regularizer rather than assuming it.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Qadam 0 — pehle dekhein ke "bigadna" ka matlab kya hai:

x badal kar (x + delta) ho gaya. Prediction mein kitna farq aaya? theta^T(x + delta) - theta^T x = theta^T delta Ghaur karein: x poora KAT gaya! Yaani farq sirf theta aur delta par hai. Ab hamein ye dekhna hai ke | theta^T delta | sab se zyada kitna ho sakta hai.

Qadam 1 — upper bound (yahan Cauchy-Schwarz lagta hai):

Cauchy-Schwarz kehti hai: | theta^T delta | <= ||theta||_2 x ||delta||_2 (Aam alfaz mein: do vectors ka dot product un ki lambaiyon ke zarb se zyada nahi ho sakta.) Aur hamein pata hai ||delta||_2 <= rho, to: | theta^T delta | <= ||theta||_2 x rho Yaani bigaad IS SE ZYADA kabhi nahi ho sakta.

Qadam 2 — magar kya ye had waqai aati hai? (Sirf "is se zyada nahi" kehna adhoora hai — dikhana padega ke itna ho sakta hai.) Ek khaas δ banayein:

delta* = rho x theta / ||theta||_2 (yaani theta ki hi taraf, magar lambai rho tak) Check 1 - qanooni hai? ||delta*||_2 = rho x ||theta||_2 / ||theta||_2 = rho HAAN Check 2 - kitna bigaad deta hai? theta^T delta* = rho x (theta^T theta) / ||theta||_2 = rho x ||theta||_2^2 / ||theta||_2 = rho x ||theta||_2 POORI HAD! To: S = rho x ||theta||_2 SABIT

(c) Had kyun haasil hoti hai: Cauchy-Schwarz mein barabari theek us waqt hoti hai jab dono vectors ek hi direction mein hon. Aur hum ne δ* jaan boojh kar θ ke saath saath banaya, is liye koi slack nahi bacha.

Aqal ki baat: farz karein koi dushman aap ki prediction bigadna chahta hai aur uske paas 0.5 ka "budget" hai. Uska behtareen daao kya hoga? Poora budget usi taraf lagana jahan model sab se zyada hilta hai — yaani θ ki taraf. Agar wo budget kisi aur taraf lagaye, to wo taraf θ ke aamood (perpendicular) hogi aur prediction mein kuch bhi farq nahi aayega — budget zaya.

Asal maqsad: ρ duniya ki cheez hai (sensor ka shor) — wo hamare haath mein nahi. To sensitivity kam karne ka ek hi rasta bacha: ‖θ‖2 chhota rakho. Yaani sum-square regularizer yahan se nikla hai — kisi ne bagair wajah thop nahi diya. (Aur isi daleel ko ℓ∞ ke saath chalayein to lasso nikalta hai.)

P-7.36 marks

A ridge model includes the offset in the penalty. House prices average ~8,000,000 rupees, and predictions come out systematically far too low. Explain the mechanism and the correct treatment of the offset.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek ridge model mein galti se offset bhi penalty ke andar daal diya gaya. Gharon ki keemat ka average taqreeban 80 lakh hai. Natija: predictions ba-qaida bohat kam aa rahi hain.

(a) Ye kyun hua?
(b) Offset ke saath theek sulook kya hai, aur kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Mechanism: regularizing the offset pulls all predictions toward zero. The offset θ1 must be ~8,000,000 to place predictions in range, so the penalty contains

lambda * theta_1^2 ~ lambda * (8,000,000)^2 = lambda * 6.4 x 10^13

This single term dwarfs everything else, so RERM buys an enormous penalty reduction by shrinking θ1 drastically, accepting a large rise in squared error as a bargain. The offset collapses toward 0, so every prediction is dragged toward 0 — the stated symptom.

Correct: do not regularize θ1 — start the penalty sum at j = 2.

Justification: the constant feature never changes, so δ1 = 0 always, and in θTδ = θ1·0 + θ2δ2 + … the offset contributes nothing to sensitivity, however large. Penalising it serves none of regularization's purpose and only adds bias.

Also: centre the target and standardize the features; then the required offset is near zero anyway.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Kya hua — offset par jurmana lagane se saari predictions zero ki taraf kheench gayin.

Kyun — numbers se dekhein: offset (θ1) ko taqreeban 80,00,000 hona zaroori hai, warna predictions sahi range mein nahi aayengi. Ab us ka jurmana nikaalein:

lambda x theta_1^2 = lambda x (80,00,000)^2 = lambda x 6.4 x 10^13

Ye ek akela number itna bara hai ke poore objective mein baqi sab kuch (saare doosre coefficients ka jurmana, aur khud squared error bhi) us ke saamne kuch bhi nahi rehta.

To RERM soch kar faisla karta hai: "agar main θ1 ko bohat chhota kar doon, to mujhe penalty mein zabardast faida hoga — haan squared error barh jaayega, magar wo faida ke muqable mein mamooli hai." Aur bas — offset gir kar 0 ke qareeb aa jaata hai, aur har prediction us ke saath 0 ki taraf kheench jaati hai.

Asal mein model ko ye kaha ja raha hai: "ghar ki keemat sifar honi chahiye, jab tak features koi aur daleel na dein." Jo bilkul bay-waqoofi hai.

(b) Theek sulook: θ1 par jurmana na lagayein — penalty ka jama j = 2 se shuru karein.

Kyun — asal daleel: regularization ka maqsad hai predictor ko kam chidchida (insensitive) banana. Ab dekhein offset is mein kitna hissa daalta hai:

Constant feature hamesha 1 rehta hai — wo kabhi badalta nahi. To uski gadbad bhi hamesha zero hai: delta_1 = 0 Aur prediction ki tabdeeli: theta^T delta = theta_1 x 0 + theta_2 x delta_2 + ... = 0 + theta_2 x delta_2 + ...

Dekha? θ1 sensitivity mein bilkul kuch hissa nahi daalta — chahe wo 80 lakh ho ya 80 crore. To us par jurmana lagana regularization ka koi maqsad poora nahi karta; wo sirf model ko jhuka deta hai.

Ek aur daleel (likhne par mark): model ka jawab is baat par depend nahi karna chahiye ke aap ne keemat rupay mein likhi ya lakh mein. Offset par jurmana lagane se ye baat toot jaati hai. Aur amali mashwara: target ko centre kar dein (average ghata dein) aur features standardize karein — phir zaroori offset khud-ba-khud 0 ke qareeb aa jaata hai aur masla paida hi nahi hota.

P-7.46 marks

A model of lifespan from healthy-behaviour scores gives a negative coefficient to daily exercise. The sample is small. Which regularizer do the lectures provide, what does it impose, and why does clipping negative coefficients to zero work poorly?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek model sehat ki aadaton se zindagi ki tawaqqo (lifespan) ka andaza lagata hai. Magar us ne "rozana exercise" ko negative coefficient de diya — yaani model keh raha hai "exercise se umar kam hoti hai". Sample chhota hai.

(a) Lectures kaunsa regularizer dete hain?
(b) Wo kya pabandi lagata hai?
(c) Aasaan tareeqa — "negative coefficients ko zero kar do" — kyun bura kaam karta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The nonnegative regularizer: φ(z) = 0 if z ≥ 0, ∞ if z < 0. With square loss this is nonnegative least squares (NNLS).

What it imposes: not really a penalty but a hard constraint written as one — "when xj increases, so must our prediction", i.e. the model is forced to be monotonically non-decreasing. It cannot assert that exercise shortens life.

Why the negative sign appeared: with a small sample and correlated behaviour scores the fit is under-determined, so noise easily produces a sign reversal. This is the prior information role of a regularizer.

NNLS loss = 0.59 LS loss = 0.30 heuristic (LS then clip) = 15.05 heuristic is 25x worse than NNLS and 50x worse than LS

Why clipping fails: least squares chooses all coefficients jointly, and with correlated features a negative coefficient is often offsetting a large positive one. Zeroing it unilaterally destroys that balance while every other coefficient stays at a now-wrong value — nothing re-optimizes. The fix is to put the constraint inside the optimization.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Jawab: nonnegative regularizer

phi(z) = 0 agar z >= 0 (koi jurmana nahi) phi(z) = infinity agar z < 0 (bilkul MANA)

Square loss ke saath isay NNLS (nonnegative least squares) kehte hain.

(b) Ye kya pabandi lagata hai: ye asal mein "jurmana" nahi, sakht pabandi hai jo jurmane ki shakal mein likhi gayi. ∞ ka matlab hai "ye bilkul mumkin nahi".

Asar ye hota hai: jab bhi koi feature barhe, prediction bhi barhni chahiye (ya kam az kam barabar rahe) — ghatni bilkul nahi chahiye. Yaani model ko ye kehne ki ijaazat hi nahi rehti ke "exercise se umar kam hoti hai".

Negative nishaan pehle kyun aaya: sample chhota hai aur sehat ki aadatein ek doosre se juri hui hain (jo exercise karta hai wo achha khaata bhi hai). Aise mein data poori tarah faisla nahi kar paata, aur mehez shor se nishaan ulat sakta hai. Yehi regularizer ka doosra kirdaar hai — prior information: hum data se pehle hi jaante hain ke exercise ka asar negative nahi ho sakta, aur wo ilm model mein daal dete hain.

(c) "Zero kar do" wala tareeqa kyun fail hota hai

Tareeqa ye hai: pehle aam least squares hal karein, phir jo coefficients negative aaye unko zero kar dein. Lectures ke numbers:

NNLS ka loss = 0.59 LS ka loss (bagair pabandi) = 0.30 "LS phir clip" ka loss = 15.05 Yaani ye aasaan tareeqa NNLS se 25 GUNA bura hai!

Kyun — asal wajah: least squares saare coefficients mila kar, ek saath chunta hai — wo ek doosre ka tawaazun (balance) karte hain. Jab features juri hui hon, to ek negative coefficient aksar kisi bade positive coefficient ki ziyadati ko kaat raha hota hai.

Ab aap us negative wale ko akele zero kar dein — to tawaazun toot gaya, magar baqi saare coefficients apni purani values par baithe rahe, jo ab ghalat hain. Kuch dobara adjust nahi hua. Natija: model bay-tarteeb ho gaya aur loss aasman par.

Theek tareeqa: pabandi ko optimization ke andar rakhein, taake saare coefficients ek saath, pabandi ko maante hue fit hon. Phir jab ek coefficient ko zero rakhna pade, to baqi khud adjust ho kar us ki kami poori kar dete hain. Isi liye NNLS ka 0.59 asal LS ke 0.30 ke qareeb hai, jabke clipping ka 15.05 kahin door.

P-7.56 marks

For ridge, lasso and the ℓ0.5 regularizer, compute the derivative of the per-coefficient penalty at θj = 1, 0.25, 0.01. Rank them by sparsifying strength and state the price paid by the strongest.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Teen regularizers ke liye penalty ka derivative nikaalein (θj = 1, 0.25, 0.01 par):

ridge = z²  ·  lasso = z  ·  ℓ0.5 = √z

Phin unko sparsity ki taqat ke hisaab se tarteeb dein, aur batayein sab se taqatwar wala kis qeemat par aata hai.

(Derivative yahan asal mein wo "zor" hai jo coefficient ko zero ki taraf dhakel raha hai.)

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

derivatives: ridge 2z lasso 1 L_0.5 0.5/sqrt(z) theta_j ridge lasso L_0.5 ------- -------- ------- -------- 1.00 2.00 1.00 0.50 0.25 0.50 1.00 1.00 0.01 0.02 1.00 5.00 trend: DECREASES CONSTANT INCREASES to zero without bound

Reading it: the derivative is the force pushing the coefficient to zero, and what matters is its behaviour as θj → 0.

  • Ridge: the force evaporates (2.00 → 0.02) → no sparsity.
  • Lasso: the force is constant right down to zero, and the kink pins the coefficient there → sparsity.
  • ℓ0.5: the force grows (0.50 → 5.00, and → ∞) → strong sparsity.

Ranking: ℓ0.5 > lasso > ridge (which gives none).

Price paid by ℓ0.5: it is not convex — the very growing derivative that sparsifies means the penalty curves the wrong way. ERM may have multiple local minima, the result depends on initialisation, and there is no optimality certificate (the lectures call it heuristic). Also (Σ|θj|0.5)² is not even a norm. Lasso is the practical choice: the smallest power still convex and the largest power still sparsifying.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Derivatives: ridge → 2z  ·  lasso → 1  ·  ℓ0.5 → 0.5/√z

theta_j ridge lasso L_0.5 (2z) (1) (0.5/sqrt z) ------- ------- ------ ------------ 1.00 2.00 1.00 0.50 0.25 0.50 1.00 1.00 0.01 0.02 1.00 5.00 rujhaan: GHATTA PAKKA BARHTA hai rehta jaata hai

Table padhne ka tareeqa: derivative wo dhakka hai jo coefficient ko zero ki taraf dhakel raha hai. Aur asal cheez ye nahi hai ke 1.00 par dhakka kitna hai — asal cheez ye hai ke zero ke paas pohonchte hue dhakke ka kya haal hota hai. Kyunki faisla waheen hota hai: "aur chhota karoon ya ruk jaaoon?"

Ridge: dhakka khatam ho jaata hai (2.00 → 0.50 → 0.02). Yaani coefficient jab chhota ho gaya, to usay aur chhota karne ka faida hi khatam. Is liye wo zero ke qareeb aa kar ruk jaata hai. Koi sparsity nahi.

Lasso: dhakka pakka 1 hi rehta hai — aakhri lamhe tak. Aur zero par jo nukeela mod (kink) hai, wo coefficient ko waheen pakad leta hai. Sparsity milti hai.

ℓ0.5: dhakka barhta jaata hai (0.50 → 1.00 → 5.00, aur zero par ∞). Yaani coefficient jitna zero ke qareeb aata hai, utna zor se dhakela jaata hai! Bohat zyada sparsity.

Tarteeb: ℓ0.5 > lasso > ridge (ridge bilkul nahi deta).

Sab se taqatwar wala kis qeemat par aata hai: wo convex nahi hai.

Kyun: wohi barhta hua dhakka, jo sparsity deta hai, wohi penalty ko ulti taraf moud deta hai. Natija: kai gaddhe, jawab shuruati guess par depend, aur optimality ka koi saboot nahi. Isi liye lecture usay sirf heuristic kehta hai. (Aur ek technical baat: (Σ|θj|0.5)² asal mein norm bhi nahi hai.)

Yaad rakhein — lasso kyun amali chunaav hai: ℓ1 ek sarhad par baitha hai. Wo sab se chhoti power hai jo phir bhi convex hai, aur sab se badi power hai jo phir bhi sparsity deti hai. Yaani wo woh akela point hai jahan dono khoobiyan mojood hain — isi liye Tibshirani ka 1994 ka lasso itna mash-hoor hua.

P-7.66 marks

60 features, ~50 strongly correlated with one another and all genuinely relevant. Lasso selects an apparently arbitrary 12, and the selection changes on resampling. Explain, and state what to use instead.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

60 features hain. Un mein se taqreeban 50 ek doosre se bohat jure hue hain (yaani taqreeban ek hi baat batate hain) — aur sab waqai kaam ke hain. Lasso un mein se bay-tarteeb 12 chun leta hai, aur data dobara sample karne par mukhtalif 12 chunta hai.

(a) Lasso aisa kyun kar raha hai?
(b) Is ki jagah kya use karein?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Why lasso does this: when features carry nearly the same information, the empirical risk barely cares which one gets the weight — but the penalties differ:

Total weight 1.0 across two near-identical features A and B: L1 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 1.0 EQUAL L2 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 0.50 SPLITTING CHEAPER

So ℓ1 is indifferent between concentrating and splitting while ℓ2 strictly prefers splitting. Lasso therefore picks one representative and zeroes the rest — and which one is decided by noise-level differences, so the selection is unstable.

Two clarifications: predictive accuracy may be fine, since the selected feature proxies for the group — the failure is one of interpretation. And since all 50 are genuinely relevant, sparsity is undesirable here — the opposite of OHT Q-7.

Use instead: ridge if all 50 are truly relevant — it distributes weight across correlated features, which is stabler. Elastic net (ℓ1 + ℓ2) if some sparsity is still wanted.

Rule: many irrelevant features → lasso. Many relevant, correlated → ridge. Both → elastic net.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Lasso aisa kyun karta hai — numbers se dekhein.

Farz karein do features A aur B taqreeban ek hi baat batate hain, aur model ko kul 1.0 weight baantna hai. Error ko farq hi nahi padta ke kis ko mile — magar penalty ko padta hai:

LASSO (L1) ke hisaab se: sab A ko do : |1.0| + |0.0| = 1.0 aadha aadha : |0.5| + |0.5| = 1.0 <-- BARABAR! RIDGE (L2) ke hisaab se: sab A ko do : 1.0^2 + 0.0^2 = 1.00 aadha aadha : 0.5^2 + 0.5^2 = 0.50 <-- BAANTNA SASTA!

Yehi poora jawab hai. Lasso ko baantne aur ek jagah jama karne mein koi farq nahi — dono ka jurmana barabar. To uske paas duplicates rakhne ki koi wajah nahi, aur wo ek numainda chun kar baqi ko zero kar deta hai (kyunki kam features rakhna aur koi faida de deta hai).

Aur kaunsa chunta hai? Jo shor ki wajah se baal barabar aage nikal gaya. Agle sample mein shor badal jaata hai, to doosra jeet jaata hai. Isi liye chunaav badalta rehta hai. Ye lasso ki jaani-pehchani kamzori hai, koi bug nahi.

Do baatein saaf karein:

  1. Accuracy theek bhi ho sakti hai! Kyunki chuna hua feature poore group ki numaindagi kar raha hai. Yaani prediction mein masla nahi — masla samajhne/bayaan karne mein hai. Hargiz ye natija na nikaalein ke baqi 48 features bay-kaar hain.
  2. Aur yaad rahe: sawal keh raha hai sab 50 waqai kaam ke hain. To yahan sparsity chahiye hi nahi — ye OHT Q-7 ka bilkul ulta case hai.

(b) Is ki jagah kya:

  • Ridge — agar waqai sab 50 kaam ke hain. Ridge weight baant deta hai (oopar ka hisaab dekhein), jo zyada stable hai aur zyada imaandar bhi: wo maan leta hai ke poora group mil kar signal de raha hai.
  • Elastic net (ℓ1 + ℓ2) — agar thodi sparsity bhi chahiye. ℓ1 wala hissa bay-kaar features nikaal dega, aur ℓ2 wala hissa juri hui kaam ki features ko sath rakhega (isay "grouping effect" kehte hain).
  • Ya pehle redundancy kam kar dein — 50 ko mila kar PCA se kuch components bana lein, ya ek maani-khez "index" bana lein.

Yaad rakhein — faisle ka qanoon: bohat se bay-kaar features → lasso. Bohat se kaam ke, magar ek doosre se jure features → ridge. Dono soortein ek saath → elastic net.

P-7.76 marks

The lectures advise choosing λ to minimise test error "and maybe a little larger…". Explain the three distinct reasons for taking λ larger than the apparent minimiser.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Lecture kehta hai: λ wo chunein jo test error sab se kam kare — "aur shayad thoda bara…". Wo teen alag alag wajuhat batayein ke λ ko minimum se bara lena kyun behtar hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

1. The validation curve is itself noisy. Each point is estimated from a finite sample, so taking the arg min over many candidates selects partly for favourable noise at that λ. Near the bottom the curve is nearly flat, so a whole range is statistically indistinguishable. Choosing on the larger side makes it robust. (Formalised as the one standard error rule.)

2. Larger λ gives a less sensitive predictor. λ controls ‖θ‖, which controls worst-case sensitivity — and "insensitive predictors often generalize well". The validation set measures today's distribution, but deployment brings feature noise and mild drift it never exhibited.

3. Larger λ gives a simpler, sparser model. With lasso it zeroes more coefficients. If λ can be raised with no meaningful accuracy loss you gain: fewer features to collect and maintain, a model that can be explained and audited, and lower variance.

Caution: this is bounded, not licence to over-regularize — push far beyond and θ → 0, leaving only the offset (underfitting).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Wajah 1 — wo curve khud bharosay ke qabil nahi hai.

Validation error ka har point mehdood data se naapa gaya hai, to us mein shor hai. Aur jaise P-2.3 mein dekha, bohat se candidates mein se sab se kam uthane ka matlab hai ke aap ne thoda "khush-naseeb" bhi uthaya. Aur ek baat: curve ke neeche wala hissa aksar taqreeban flat hota hai — yaani ek poori range ke errors statistically ek jaise hain, aur un mein se "sab se kam" ka chunaav mehez ittefaq hai. To us flat hisse ke bari taraf se chunna zyada mazboot faisla hai. (Statistics mein isay "one standard error rule" kehte hain.)

Wajah 2 — bara λ model ko kam chidchida banata hai.

λ ‖θ‖ ko qaabu karta hai, aur ‖θ‖ sensitivity ko (P-7.2 dekhein). Aur Lecture-5 ka buniyadi usool: "kam chidchide predictors aksar acha generalize karte hain."

Ab ghaur karein: validation set aaj ke data ko naapta hai. Magar deployment mein features mein shor aayega, sensors purane ho jaayenge, duniya thodi badal jaayegi — aur ye sab us validation set mein mojood nahi tha. Thoda bara λ is anjaan nuqsan se bachaav khareed leta hai, aur badle mein validation error mein mamooli izafa hota hai. Sauda faide ka hai.

Wajah 3 — bara λ saada aur chhota model deta hai.

Lasso ke saath bara λ zyada coefficients zero kar deta hai. Agar accuracy mein koi maani-khez kami ke bagair λ barh sakta hai, to ye faide milte hain:

  • Kam features sambhalne padte hain — production mein har feature ek zimmedari hai (data jama karna, quality check karna, kharaab hone par theek karna).
  • Model samjhaya ja sakta hai — aur medicine, bank aur insurance mein ye qanooni zaroorat hoti hai.
  • Variance kam — kyunki bay-kaar feature ka har coefficient mehez shor par fit hota hai.

Barabar accuracy wale do models mein se saada chunna chahiye.

Ehtiyaat (ye bhi likh dein): ye ek mehdood tabdeeli hai, over-regularize karne ki ijaazat nahi. Bohat aage barh jaayein to θ → 0 ho jaata hai aur sirf offset bachta hai — yaani constant predictor, poora underfitting. Lecture ka lafz "a little larger" hai, aur kitna "little" — ye curve khud bata deta hai.

Aakhri imtihaan

Mock OHT-1 — asli format mein

60 minute ki ghadi lagayein, kitaab band karein. 10 sawal, 6 marks har ek, kul 60. Jawab chaar line mein. (Yahan bhi har sawal ke neeche Roman Urdu tarjuma hai — magar koshish karein pehle English hi se samajhein, kyunki paper mein Urdu nahi hogi.)

M-16 marks

A music service has 40 million unlabelled listening logs and wants to group users by taste. A second team wants to predict, per user, cancellation next month, using 3 years of labelled records. State the learning type for each, and explain why a random train/test split is acceptable for one but not the other.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek music app ke paas 4 crore sunne ke records hain, jin par koi label nahi, aur wo users ko pasand ke hisaab se groups mein baantna chahti hai. Doosri team 3 saal ke labelled records se andaza lagana chahti hai ke kaun agle mahine subscription band karega.

(a) Dono ka learning type batayein.
(b) Random train/test split ek ke liye theek kyun hai aur doosre ke liye nahi?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Task 1: unsupervised (clustering) — no labels, output hidden structure. Task 2: supervised binary classification, specifically forecasting, since a future value is predicted from past ones.

Random split: unacceptable for Task 2. The deployment question is "what happens next month", but a random split interleaves test months among training months, so the model sees data from after each test point — look-ahead bias / leakage. Use temporal (forward-chaining) validation.

For Task 1 there is no target to predict, so the framing is less central — but if cluster stability is evaluated, split by user, since multiple logs per user would otherwise leak (identity leakage, broken independence).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kaam 1: unsupervised (clustering) — koi label nahi, groups khud banane hain.
Kaam 2: supervised classification, aur khaas tor par forecasting (kyunki guzre waqt se aane wale waqt ka andaza).

Random split — Kaam 2 ke liye bilkul GHALAT.

Kyun: asal sawal hai "agle mahine kya hoga". Magar random split test ke mahine training ke mahinon ke beech beech bo deta hai. To model har test point ke baad ka data bhi dekh leta hai — yaani usay future ki jhalak mil jaati hai. Isi ko look-ahead bias / leakage kehte hain. Ilaaj: temporal (forward-chaining) validation — pehle mahinon par train, agle par test, aur aage barhte jaayein.

Kaam 1 mein koi target hi nahi, to split ka sawal itna markazi nahi — magar agar clusters ki pakkaai (stability) naapni ho to split user ke hisaab se karein, warna ek user ke kai records split ke dono taraf chale jaayenge (yehi identity leakage hai, P-2.1 wala masla).

M-26 marks

A warehouse predicts item pick-time from 4 raw features. It systematically under-predicts for heavy items on high shelves, but predicts well for heavy items on low shelves and light items on high shelves. Identify the problem, write the corrected feature map, and state what property is preserved.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek warehouse 4 raw features se andaza lagata hai ke cheez uthane mein kitna waqt lagega. Masla: jo cheezein bhaari hain AUR oonchi shelf par hain, un ke liye model ba-qaida kam waqt batata hai. Magar bhaari+neechi theek, aur halki+oonchi bhi theek.

(a) Masla kya hai?
(b) Theek feature map likhein.
(c) Kaunsi khoobi barqaraar rehti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Problem: a missing interaction — underfitting from an inadequate feature map. The model is additive, so weight and height each contribute a fixed independent amount. But only the combination is mispredicted, which means the effect of weight depends on height — something an additive function cannot represent. The systematic residual confirms underfitting.

phi(u) = ( 1 , weight , height , weight x height )

The interaction gives the weight-effect a second slope that applies only at height: slope in weight becomes θ2 low down and θ2 + θ4·height higher up.

Property preserved: linear in θ — the product is just another column, so the risk stays convex and least squares applies unchanged.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Masla: interaction feature missing hai — yaani na-kaafi features ki wajah se underfitting.

Kyun — shikayat ko dhyan se padhein: bhaari+neechi theek. Halki+oonchi theek. Sirf bhaari AUR oonchi — yaani sirf milaap galat. Iska matlab saaf hai: wazan ka asar bulandi par depend karta hai. (Aqal se bhi theek lagta hai — bhaari cheez oonchi shelf se utarna seerhi ya machine maangta hai.)

Magar additive model mein wazan ka asar pakka hota hai, chahe shelf oonchi ho ya neechi. Wo ye baat bayaan hi nahi kar sakta.

(b) Hal: dono ka zarb daal dein

phi(u) = ( 1 , weight , height , weight x height )

Ab chauthi feature wazan ko ek doosri dhalaan de deti hai jo sirf bulandi par chalti hai: neeche dhalaan θ2, aur oopar θ2 + θ4·height.

(c) Barqaraar khoobi: model θ mein linear hi rehta hai. Zarb (weight × height) bas ek naya column hai — uska number nikaal kar table mein daal diya. To problem convex rehti hai aur least squares bagair kisi tabdeeli chalta rahega.

Yaad rakhein: lafz "systematically" hamesha underfitting ki nishani hai (overfitting ki nahi). Aur jab sirf do cheezon ka milaap galat ho → interaction daalein.

M-36 marks

Given y = (5, 8, 8, 11, 38) minutes, compute the optimal constant under square loss and absolute loss. Which would you quote to a customer, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Delivery ke waqt: y = (5, 8, 8, 11, 38) minute. (38 ek outlier hai.)

(a) Square loss ka behtareen akela number nikaalein.
(b) Absolute loss ka.
(c) Customer ko kaunsa batayenge, aur kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

SQUARE -> MEAN = (5+8+8+11+38)/5 = 70/5 = 14 ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN: n=5 odd, median = y_3 = 8 check n_1 = #{y<8} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 ok ; n_2 = #{y>8} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 ok

Observation: the mean (14) lies above four of the five points, dragged up by the single 38; the median (8) sits inside the cluster. Moving one value by 24 shifts the mean by 24/5 = 4.8 and the median not at all.

Which to quote: of the two, the median (8) better describes typical performance, being robust. But for a customer promise neither is right — quoting 8 means about half the deliveries are late. The correct tool is tilted absolute loss with τ > ½, whose optimum is the τ-quantile: here the 0.75-quantile is 11 minutes (0.6 ≤ 0.75 ≤ 0.8 ✓). Quote 11.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) SQUARE -> MEAN = (5+8+8+11+38)/5 = 70/5 = 14 (b) ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN: 5 points (taaq), to teesra = 8 Tasdeeq: 8 se chhote = 1 (sirf 5) -> 0.2 <= 0.5 theek 8 se bare = 2 (11, 38) -> 0.4 <= 0.5 theek

Ghaur karein: mean (14) paanch mein se chaar points se oopar hai! Usay akele 38 ne kheench liya. Median (8) jhund ke beech baitha hai.

(c) Customer ko kya batayein: in do mein se median (8) aam performance ko behtar bayaan karta hai (robust hai).

Magar asal jawab ye hai ke customer ke liye dono hi ghalat hain!

Kyun: median ka matlab hi ye hai ke aadhe points us se oopar hain. To 8 minute ka wada karne se taqreeban aadhi deliveries late hongi. Customer naraz.

Theek hathiyar: tilted absolute loss, τ > ½ — jiska jawab τ-quantile hota hai. Yahan 0.75-quantile nikaalein:

theta = 11 try karein: 11 se chhote = 3 (5, 8, 8) -> 0.6 11 se bare = 1 (38) -> 0.2 range = [0.6 , 0.8] 0.75 andar hai => jawab 11 To customer ko 11 minute batayein (sirf ~25% late hongi).

Yaad rakhein: "behtareen number" aur "customer ko batane wala number" do mukhtalif cheezein hain. Pehla statistics ka sawal hai, doosra business ka — aur business ka jawab quantile deta hai.

M-46 marks

A vibration monitor predicts bearing wear. A loose cable produced 7 wildly wrong readings out of 900, and the team used squared loss. Explain the poor fit using the penalty derivative, and recommend a loss, justifying it over the alternatives.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek machine ki ghisaawat (wear) ka model. Ek dheeli cable ki wajah se 900 mein se 7 readings bilkul ghalat aa gayin. Team ne squared loss use kiya aur fit bura aaya.

(a) Derivative ke zariye samjhayein ke aisa kyun hua.
(b) Ek loss tajweez karein — aur batayein baqi options par usay tarjeeh kyun di.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Mechanism: under square loss each point's gradient pull is p′(r) = 2r, which grows without bound. A reading wrong by a factor of 100 contributes 10,000× the loss and 100× the pull, so although the 7 bad readings are under 0.8% of the data they can dominate the average, and ERM shifts θ toward them — degrading all 893 valid readings.

Recommend Huber: r² for |r| ≤ α, α(2|r| − α) beyond. The derivative saturates at 2α, so each corrupted reading exerts a bounded pull however extreme, while the 893 good readings keep the efficient quadratic treatment. α by validation, ~1–2× the MAD.

Over the alternatives: absolute loss is robust and convex but penalises small residuals relatively more and is kinked at 0. Log-Huber is more robust but not convex → local minima. Huber is the only one robust, convex and smooth.

Also: since the cause is a known loose cable, those 7 records are provably invalid and should simply be removed.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Kyun hua — derivative se:

Square loss mein har point ka zor p′(r) = 2r hai — aur ye bay-had barhta hai. To jo reading 100 guna ghalat hai:

uska loss = 100^2 = 10,000 guna zyada uska ZOR = 100 guna zyada

Natija: wo 7 readings data ke 0.8% se bhi kam hain, magar average loss mein un ka hissa baqi 893 se zyada ho jaata hai! To ERM θ ko unhi ki taraf khisaka deta hai (taake un ke bade terms kam hon), aur saari 893 sahi readings ki prediction kharab ho jaati hai.

(b) Tajweez: Huber loss

|r| <= alpha par : r^2 (square jaisa) |r| > alpha par : alpha(2|r| - alpha) (seedhi line)

Kyun: α ke baad uska derivative 2α par ruk jaata hai. To har kharab reading mehdood zor lagati hai — chahe wo 100 guna ghalat ho ya 10,000 guna. Aur jo 893 sahi readings hain, un par wohi faidemand square wala sulook barqaraar rehta hai. α validation se, taqreeban MAD ka 1–2 guna.

Baqi options par tarjeeh kyun:

  • Absolute loss: robust aur convex hai, magar chhote residuals par uska jurmana nisbatan zyada hai (to saaf data utna kas kar fit nahi hota) aur 0 par nukeela mod hai.
  • Log-Huber: zyada robust hai magar convex nahi → kai gaddhe, jawab shuruati guess par depend.
  • Huber: akela hai jo robust + convex + smooth — teeno hai.

Extra mark: ye zaroor likhein ke kyunki wajah ek maaloom dheeli cable hai, wo 7 records sabit shuda ghalat hain — unko seedha nikaal dena chahiye. Robust loss un ke liye nahi, bache hue anjaan shor ke liye hai.

M-56 marks

A genomics group has 20,000 gene-expression features and 180 samples. Ridge returns 20,000 non-zero coefficients. Why, which regularizer instead (with formula), and two benefits beyond accuracy?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek genomics team ke paas 20,000 gene features hain magar sirf 180 samples. Ridge saare 20,000 coefficients non-zero de deta hai.

(a) Kyun?
(b) Is ki jagah kaunsa regularizer (formula ke saath)?
(c) Accuracy ke ilawa do faide?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Why no zeros: the ridge derivative is 2θj, which itself tends to 0 as θj → 0. Driving a small coefficient to exactly zero gains almost nothing in penalty (0.01 → 0.00 saves only 0.0001) while always raising empirical risk. Geometrically the ℓ2 ball is round with no vertices on the axes. Ridge shrinks, it does not select.

use the LASSO : r(theta) = ||theta||_1 = |theta_1| + ... + |theta_d|

Its derivative is ±1 — constant down to zero — and the kink at 0 pins coefficients there. Note d/n = 20,000/180 ≈ 111, so regularization is not optional.

Two benefits: (1) interpretability — a shortlist of ~30 genes is a biologically testable hypothesis; 20,000 coefficients are unreadable. (2) cheaper deployment — a clinical assay need only measure the selected genes.

Caution: with n = 180 expect unstable selection, so repeat across resamples and report consistently selected genes.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Zero kyun nahi aate: ridge ka derivative 2θj hai, jo θj chhota hone par khud chhota ho jaata hai. Numbers se:

0.01 ko 0.00 karne se penalty mein bachat = 0.01^2 = 0.0001 (yaani taqreeban kuch bhi nahi) Magar us ke badle error HAMESHA barhta hai. => sauda ghaate ka => coefficient chhota reh jaata hai, zero nahi hota

Geometry se: ℓ2 ka ilaaqa gol hai — axes par koi nukeela kona nahi. Yaani ridge dabaata hai, chunta nahi. (20,000 non-zero rehna bilkul tawaqqo ke mutabiq hai, koi kharabi nahi.)

(b) Is ki jagah: LASSO

r(theta) = ||theta||_1 = |theta_1| + |theta_2| + ... + |theta_d|

Kyun: iska derivative ±1 hai — zero tak pakka — aur zero par jo nukeela mod hai wo coefficient ko waheen pakad leta hai.

Aur dhyan rahe: 20,000 / 180 ≈ 111 — yaani har sample ke badle 111 features! Yahan regularization marzi ki baat nahi, lazmi hai.

(c) Do faide (accuracy ke ilawa):

  1. Samajh mein aata hai. 30 genes ki chhoti list ek aisi hypothesis hai jo lab mein test ki ja sakti hai — biologist us par kaam shuru kar sakta hai. 20,000 coefficients ki list ko koi insaan padh hi nahi sakta.
  2. Sasta deployment. Clinical test mein sirf chune hue 30 genes naapne padenge, poora transcriptome nahi — yaani kharcha aur waqt dono bohat kam.

Ehtiyaat (likhne par mark): n = 180 par chunaav be-aitbaar hoga (P-7.6 wala masla). Is liye data ko kai baar dobara sample kar kar lasso chalayein, aur sirf wo genes report karein jo baar baar chune jaate hain.

M-66 marks

An insurance model trained on one province is deployed nationally. A second model is trained on households where neighbours' outcomes are strongly correlated. Name the technique for each and state precisely which part of i.i.d. fails.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(i) Ek insurance model sirf ek soobe ke data par bana aur poore mulk mein deploy ho gaya.
(ii) Doosra model aise gharon par bana jahan parosiyon ke natije ek doosre se bohat jure hue hain.

Dono ke liye technique ka naam dein, aur theek theek batayein ke i.i.d. ka kaunsa hissa tootta hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Case 1 — national deployment: transfer learning (domain adaptation). The part that fails is "identically distributed" — different roads, weather, vehicle mix and repair costs. Task unchanged → covariate shift. Fix: fine-tune on other provinces, re-weight samples, and validate on out-of-province data only.

Case 2 — correlated neighbours: collective classification. The part that fails is independence — outcomes are spatially autocorrelated (shared flood plains, crime rates, weather), so one household's outcome informs another's. Predict linked households jointly, and split validation data by neighbourhood, not by household.

The distinction: distribution changed → transfer learning; points linked to one another → collective classification. These are the two halves of i.i.d., with different remedies.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(i) Ek soobe se poore mulk: TRANSFER LEARNING (khaas tor par domain adaptation).

Kaunsa hissa toota: "identically distributed" — yaani data ki jagah badal gayi. Doosre soobon mein sadkein alag, mausam alag, gaadiyon ki qism alag, marammat ke kharche alag. Kaam wohi hai (claim ka andaza), sirf mahaul badla → is ko covariate shift kehte hain.

Ilaaj: doosre soobon ke data par fine-tune karein, samples ko dobara weight dein, aur validation soobe se bahar ke data par karein — usi soobe ke split par hargiz nahi.

(ii) Jure hue parosi: COLLECTIVE CLASSIFICATION

Kaunsa hissa toota: independence — yaani points ek doosre se jure hue hain. Ek gali ke gharon ka paani ka nikaas ek jaisa, jurm ki shah ek jaisi, mausam ek jaisa. Agar ek ghar mein nuqsan hua to parosi mein bhi hoga.

Ilaaj: jure hue gharon ka faisla mila kar karein, aur validation data mohalle ke hisaab se baantein, ghar ke hisaab se nahi.

Yaad rakhein — do hisse, do ilaaj: jagah badli → transfer learning. Points ek doosre se jure → collective classification. Ye i.i.d. ke do alag daawe hain aur in ka ilaaj bilkul alag hai — ye farq hi sawal ka asal maqsad hai.

M-76 marks

A reservoir model predicts safe water release. Releasing too much floods downstream; too little merely wastes hydro-power. Which loss, what range for its parameter (with an explicit sign calculation), and what is the optimal constant called?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek dam ka model batata hai ke kitna paani chhodna mehfooz hai. Zyada chhod dein to neeche sailaab. Kam chhodein to sirf bijli zaya.

(a) Kaunsa loss?
(b) Uske parameter ki range — nishaan ka hisaab kar ke.
(c) Behtareen constant ko kya kehte hain?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Loss: tilted absolute loss with τ < ½.

SIGN CHECK. r = y-hat - y safe volume y = 400, model predicts y-hat = 550 r = 550 - 400 = +150 (POSITIVE) -> too much released -> FLOODING => FLOODING = over-estimation = r > 0 <- dangerous side r >= 0 costs (1-tau)|r| ; r < 0 costs tau|r| need (1 - tau) > tau => tau < 1/2 tau = 0.05 : over-release by 50 -> 0.95(50) = 47.5 under-release by 50 -> 0.05(50) = 2.5 -> 19 : 1

Optimal constant: the τ-quantile — at τ = 0.05, the 5th percentile, a deliberately conservative low release so only ~5% of decisions over-release.

Sanity check: penalising over-release should make the model release less; a small τ gives a low quantile. ✓ Caveat: a quantile loss gives asymmetry, not a hard limit; for a true flood threshold add an explicit constraint.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Loss: tilted absolute loss, aur τ aadhe se kam.

(b) Nishaan ka hisaab — numbers daal kar, andaze se nahi:

r = y-hat - y Farz karein mehfooz miqdaar y = 400 thi aur model kehta hai y-hat = 550 r = 550 - 400 = +150 (POSITIVE) Yaani zaroorat se ZYADA paani chhod diya -> SAILAAB To: SAILAAB = zyada batana = r > 0 <-- khatarnak taraf Ab kaunsa tau is taraf saza deta hai? r >= 0 ki qeemat = (1 - tau)|r| r < 0 ki qeemat = tau |r| chahiye (1 - tau) > tau => 1 > 2 tau => tau < 1/2 Check (tau = 0.05): 50 zyada chhoda -> 0.95 x 50 = 47.5 50 kam chhoda -> 0.05 x 50 = 2.5 -> 19 : 1

(c) Behtareen constant: τ-quantile. τ = 0.05 par ye 5th percentile hai — yaani jaan boojh kar bohat ehtiyaat wali kam miqdaar, taake sirf ~5% faislon mein zyada paani chhoda jaaye.

Aqal se tasdeeq (ye zaroor karein): agar hum zyada chhodne par saza laga rahe hain, to model ko kam chhodna chahiye. Aur chhota τ neecha quantile deta hai, yaani kam miqdaar. ✓ Mel kha gaya.

Ehtiyaat: quantile loss sirf na-barabari deta hai, pakki had nahi. Yaani 19:1 ke baad bhi model kabhi kabhi bohat zyada paani chhod sakta hai agar us se average loss kam ho. Asli sailaab ki had ke liye alag se constraint lagana padega. (Ye baat likhne par examiner khush hota hai.)

M-86 marks

A bookstore recommender is built from 6 million transaction records with no target column, observing P(cookbook | kitchen gadget) = 0.55. A manager insists this is "obviously supervised learning since we are predicting purchases". Settle the disagreement.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek bookstore ka recommender 60 lakh transactions se bana, jin mein koi target column nahi. Mila: jo log kitchen ka saamaan khareedte hain, un mein se 55% cooking ki kitab bhi khareedte hain.

Manager zid kar raha hai: "ye to zahir hai supervised learning hai, kyunki hum khareedari ka andaza lagaa rahe hain!" Is ikhtilaaf ka faisla karein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

As described this is association analysis — the manager is conflating "predicting something" with "supervised learning".

Not supervised: that needs labelled pairs with a designated target, and the data has no target column. The 0.55 is an association rule's confidence. Nor is it clustering: the relationships are between items, not partitions of customers.

Where the manager has a point: association analysis does produce predictions — that simply does not imply supervision. And the task could be reframed as supervised ("will this customer buy a cookbook?") with per-customer labels.

Resolution: association analysis is item-centric, label-free, and yields rules over all item pairs at once — what a catalogue-wide recommender needs, since a separate classifier per product is infeasible. Answering the question as posed: no labels exist, so association analysis.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: jaisa bataya gaya hai, ye ASSOCIATION ANALYSIS hai. Manager do mukhtalif baaton ko mila raha hai: "kuch predict karna" aur "supervised learning".

Supervised kyun nahi: us ke liye har record ke saath ek muqarrar target likha hona chahiye. Magar sawal saaf keh raha hai: koi target column nahi. Aur jo 55% ka number hai, wo sirf parchiyan gin kar nikla hai — yaani ek association rule ka confidence.

Aur ye clustering bhi nahi — kyunki rishte cheezon ke darmiyan hain (kitchen saamaan → cookbook), customers ki taqseem nahi.

Manager ki baat mein jo sahi hai: haan, association analysis waqai predictions deta hai — recommender usi par chalta hai. Magar "prediction dena" ka matlab "supervised hona" nahi hai. Ye manager ki asal ghalti hai.

Aur ye bhi sach hai ke kaam ko supervised bana sakte hain: "ye khaas customer cookbook khareedega?" — magar us ke liye har customer ke saath label aur uski tafseel chahiye, jo mojood nahi.

Faisla:

  • Association analysis: cheezon par markoz, label nahi chahiye, aur ek saath saare item jodon ke rules deta hai. Poore catalogue ke recommender ke liye yehi mumkin hai — kyunki hazaaron products ke liye hazaaron alag classifiers banana namumkin hai.
  • Supervised classification: records par markoz, target chahiye, aur har banday ke liye alag jawab deta hai.

Yaad rakhein: sawal jaisa poocha gaya us ka jawab dein — labels mojood nahi, to association analysis. (Aur ye likh dein ke reframe karne par supervised ban sakta hai — is se poora numbers milta hai.)

M-96 marks

A clinic uses 1-NN over 6 measurements including creatinine (0.5–1.2 mg/dL) and systolic blood pressure (90–200 mmHg). Training accuracy is 100%, deployment is poor. Explain both defects quantitatively and state the remedies.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek clinic 1-NN use karti hai, 6 measurements par. Un mein creatinine (range 0.5–1.2) aur blood pressure (range 90–200) shamil hain. Training par accuracy 100%, magar asal mein buri.

Dono kharabiyan numbers ke saath samjhayein aur ilaaj batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Defect 1 — 100% training accuracy is vacuous. Each point is its own nearest neighbour (distance 0), so error is 0 for any loss — from the definition, not from learning. Why k=1 overfits: each prediction rests on one point, so no averaging suppresses label noise and one misdiagnosis corrupts its whole Voronoi cell; the predictor is a step function, hence maximally sensitive.

Defect 2 — unscaled features let blood pressure dominate:

A: creatinine 0.7, BP 110 B: creatinine 1.2, BP 170 sq. distance = (0.5)^2 + (60)^2 = 0.25 + 3600 = 3600.25 BP contributes 3600/3600.25 = 99.993 % creatinine contributes 0.25/3600.25 = 0.007 %

So "nearest patient" means, to four decimals, "closest blood pressure". A 0.5 mg/dL creatinine difference — clinically normal-to-impaired renal function — is invisible. Since 1-NN has no parameters, nothing can compensate: the distance is the model. It is also unit-dependent.

Remedies: standardize every feature (training statistics only); raise k or use soft-NN with β; select by validation, never training error; consider feature selection.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kharabi 1 — 100% training accuracy KHAALI hai.

Kyun: har point ka sab se qareeb parosi khud hota hai (faasla 0). To model us ka apna jawab wapas de deta hai aur error 0 aa jaata hai — kisi bhi loss ke saath. Ye model ki tareef se nikla hai, seekhne se nahi. Ek lookup table apne hi khaano par hamesha 100% hoti hai.

k=1 overfit kyun karta hai: har jawab ek point par tika hai, to label ka shor dabane ke liye koi averaging nahi; ek ghalat diagnosis apne poore aas paas ke ilaaqe ko kharab kar deta hai; aur prediction ek "step function" hai — measurements mein zara si tabdeeli par bimaari ka jawab palat sakta hai.

Kharabi 2 — scaling na hone se blood pressure poore faasle par qabza kar leta hai.

Do patients lein: A: creatinine 0.7, BP 110 B: creatinine 1.2, BP 170 Squared faasla = (1.2 - 0.7)^2 + (170 - 110)^2 = (0.5)^2 + (60)^2 = 0.25 + 3600 = 3600.25 Hissa: BP = 3600 / 3600.25 = 99.993 % creatinine = 0.25 / 3600.25 = 0.007 %

Yaani "sab se qareeb patient" ka matlab chaar decimal tak "jis ka blood pressure sab se qareeb" ban gaya. Aur creatinine ka 0.5 ka farq — jo medically theek guurde aur kharab guurde ka faasla hai — bilkul nazar hi nahi aa raha.

Aur yaad rahe: 1-NN mein koi parameter nahi hota jo is na-barabari ko theek kar sake. Faasla hi model hai. Aur jawab unit par bhi depend karta hai (creatinine ko µmol/L mein likh dein to predictions badal jaayengi).

Ilaaj: (1) har feature standardize karein — aur statistics sirf training se lein; (2) k barhayein ya soft-NN use karein β ke saath; (3) chunaav validation se karein, training error se hargiz nahi (wo hamesha k=1 chunega); (4) 6 features ke liye feature selection par ghaur karein.

M-106 marks

State the definition of a convex function and the optimality condition for a non-differentiable convex function. Then explain why these two facts make ℓ1 both computable and sparsifying.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

(a) Convex function ki tareef dein.
(b) Un shartein likhein jo nukeele mod wale (non-differentiable) convex function ke minimum ke liye hain.
(c) Phir samjhayein ke ye do baatein mil kar ℓ1 (lasso) ko nikaalne ke qabil aur sparsity dene wala — dono kaise bana deti hain.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Definition: f(αw + (1−α)z) ≤ αf(w) + (1−α)f(z) for all w, z and α ∈ [0,1] — every chord lies on or above the graph.

Optimality without differentiability: w minimises f iff f′−(w) ≤ 0 and f′+(w) ≥ 0, where the one-sided derivatives always exist for a convex function. (For |w| at 0 they are −1 and +1, so 0 is the minimiser although f′(0) does not exist.)

Why ℓ1 is computable: ‖θ‖1 is convex, so the lasso objective is convex — every local minimum is global, the conditions are sufficient, and the solution is reproducible and efficiently computed. The kink removes the closed form but convexity preserves tractability.

Why ℓ1 is sparsifying: the kink is the mechanism. The derivative is ±1 — constant right to zero, unlike ridge's vanishing 2θj — and because it jumps from −1 to +1 at the origin, both one-sided conditions can hold exactly at θj = 0 for a whole range of data gradients: whenever the risk pull is smaller than λ, the coefficient is pinned there.

Synthesis: non-differentiability creates sparsity; convexity keeps it solvable. ℓ1 is the unique power with both.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) Tareef: graph par koi bhi do point lein aur un ke beech seedhi line (chord) khench dein — agar wo line hamesha curve ke oopar (ya us par) rahe, to function convex hai. Formal shakal:

f( alpha w + (1-alpha) z ) <= alpha f(w) + (1-alpha) f(z)

(b) Nukeele mod wale function ke liye shartein:

w minimum hai AGAR f'_-(w) <= 0 AUR f'_+(w) >= 0 (ye dono "ek taraf ke" derivatives convex function mein HAMESHA mojood hote hain, chahe aam derivative mojood na ho) Misaal: |w| ke liye 0 par f'_- = -1 aur f'_+ = +1 -1 <= 0 theek, +1 >= 0 theek => 0 par minimum hai (halaanke wahan aam derivative mojood hi nahi!)

(c) Pehle: ℓ1 "nikaalne ke qabil" kyun hai

‖θ‖1 convex hai, to poora lasso objective convex hai. Is ke teen faide milte hain: jo gaddha mila wohi sab se gehra hai; shartein kaafi hain (yaani saabit kiya ja sakta hai); aur computer usay efficiently hal kar leta hai. Lecture ka jumla yaad karein: "formula nahi hai, magar phir bhi hum usay efficiently nikaal lete hain, kyunki wo convex hai." Yaani mod ne formula khatam kiya, magar convexity ne hal nikalna bacha liya.

Ab: ℓ1 sparsity kyun deta hai — aur yahan asal kamaal hai

Nukeela mod masla nahi hai — wohi asal mechanism hai! Do wajuhat:

  1. Dhakka khatam nahi hota. Derivative ±1 hai — zero tak pakka. Ridge ka 2θj zero ke paas khud mar jaata hai; lasso ka nahi.
  2. Mod par derivative "chhalaang" lagata hai — −1 se seedha +1. Aur isi chhalaang ki wajah se dono shartein θj = 0 par hi poori ho sakti hain — aur ye data ke gradients ki poori range ke liye hota hai. Yaani jab bhi data ka zor λ se chhota ho, coefficient waheen jam jaata hai. Smooth penalty mein aisa "jamna" mumkin hi nahi, kyunki wahan derivative ko theek theek balance karna padta hai.

Khulasa (ye line jawab mein zaroor likhein): nukeela mod sparsity paida karta hai; aur convexity usay hal karne qabil rakhti hai. ℓ1 wo akeli power hai jis mein dono khoobiyan hain — ℓ2 convex hai magar smooth (sparsity nahi), aur ℓ0.5 mein mod hai magar convex nahi (sirf heuristic).

Mock ke baad — khud ko check karein

Har sawal par chaar cheezein dekhein. Chaaron hain to 6/6; sirf naam likha hai to 2/6.

  • Naam liya? Technique ka theek naam, pehli line mein.
  • Mechanism diya? Formula ya derivative — p′(r) = 2r, 2θj → 0, ρ‖θ‖1.
  • Scenario se joda? Sawal ke apne lafz aur numbers (dheeli cable, 20,000 genes, creatinine 0.5–1.2).
  • Sifarish ki? Kya karein + "choose α / λ / k by validation" — taqreeban hamesha fit hota hai.
